# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Pebble Development Environment

Pebble is a LevelDB/RocksDB inspired key-value store focused on
performance and internal usage by CockroachDB.

## Testing
```bash
go test -tags invariants .
go test -tags invariants ./...
```

## Regenerating test files
```bash
go test -tags invariants ./<package> --rewrite
```

## Linting
```bash
go test -tags invariants ./internal/lint"
```

## When generating PRs and commit records

- Follow the format:
  - Separate the subject from the body with a blank line.
  - Use the body of the commit record to explain what existed before your change, what you changed, and why.
  - Prefix the subject line with the package in which the bulk of the changes occur.
  - For multi-commit PRs, summarize each commit in the PR record.
  - Do not include a test plan unless explicitly asked by the user.

## Interaction Style

* Be direct and honest.
* Skip unnecessary acknowledgments.
* Correct me when I'm wrong and explain why.
* Suggest better alternatives if my ideas can be improved.
* Focus on accuracy and efficiency.
* Challenge my assumptions when needed.
* Prioritize quality information and directness.

// Copyright 2018 The LevelDB-Go and Pebble Authors. All rights reserved. Use
// of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be found in
// the LICENSE file.

package pebble

import (
	"fmt"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"time"

	"github.com/cockroachdb/crlib/crhumanize"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/crlib/crtime"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/errors"
	errorsjoin "github.com/cockroachdb/errors/join"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/base"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/humanize"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/invariants"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/manifest"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/objstorage"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/objstorage/remote"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/vfs"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/redact"
)

// TableNum is an identifier for a table within a database.
type TableNum = base.TableNum

// TableInfo exports the manifest.TableInfo type.
type TableInfo = manifest.TableInfo

func tablesTotalSize(tables []TableInfo) uint64 {
	var size uint64
	for i := range tables {
		size += tables[i].Size
	}
	return size
}

func formatFileNums(tables []TableInfo) string {
	var buf strings.Builder
	for i := range tables {
		if i > 0 {
			buf.WriteString(" ")
		}
		buf.WriteString(tables[i].FileNum.String())
	}
	return buf.String()
}

// DataCorruptionInfo contains the information for a DataCorruption event.
type DataCorruptionInfo struct {
	// Path of the file that is corrupted. For remote files the path starts with
	// "remote://".
	Path     string
	IsRemote bool
	// Locator is only set when IsRemote is true (note that an empty Locator is
	// valid even then).
	Locator remote.Locator
	// Bounds indicates the keyspace range that is affected.
	Bounds base.UserKeyBounds
	// Details of the error. See cockroachdb/error for how to format with or
	// without redaction.
	Details error
}

func (i DataCorruptionInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)
}

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i DataCorruptionInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	w.Printf("on-disk corruption: %s", redact.Safe(i.Path))
	if i.IsRemote {
		w.Printf(" (remote locator %q)", redact.Safe(i.Locator))
	}
	w.Printf("; bounds: %s; details: %+v", i.Bounds.String(), i.Details)
}

// LevelInfo contains info pertaining to a particular level.
type LevelInfo struct {
	Level  int
	Tables []TableInfo
	Blobs  []BlobFileInfo
	Score  float64
}

func (i LevelInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)
}

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i LevelInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	blobInfo := redact.SafeString("")
	if len(i.Blobs) > 0 {
		pluralBlob := redact.SafeString("s")
		if len(i.Blobs) == 1 {
			pluralBlob = ""
		}
		blobInfo = redact.SafeString(fmt.Sprintf(" blob%s [%s] (%s)",
			pluralBlob, formatBlobFileNums(i.Blobs), humanize.Bytes.Uint64(blobsTotalSize(i.Blobs))))
	}
	w.Printf("L%d [%s] (%s)%s Score=%.2f",
		redact.Safe(i.Level),
		redact.Safe(formatFileNums(i.Tables)),
		redact.Safe(humanize.Bytes.Uint64(tablesTotalSize(i.Tables))),
		blobInfo,
		redact.Safe(i.Score))
}

// BlobFileCreateInfo contains the info for a blob file creation event.
type BlobFileCreateInfo struct {
	JobID int
	// Reason is the reason for the table creation: "compacting", "flushing", or
	// "ingesting".
	Reason  string
	Path    string
	FileNum base.DiskFileNum
}

func (i BlobFileCreateInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)
}

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i BlobFileCreateInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	w.Printf("[JOB %d] %s: blob file created %s",
		redact.Safe(i.JobID), redact.Safe(i.Reason), i.FileNum)
}

// BlobFileDeleteInfo contains the info for a blob file deletion event.
type BlobFileDeleteInfo struct {
	JobID   int
	Path    string
	FileNum base.DiskFileNum
	Err     error
}

func (i BlobFileDeleteInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)
}

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i BlobFileDeleteInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	if i.Err != nil {
		w.Printf("[JOB %d] blob file delete error %s: %s",
			redact.Safe(i.JobID), i.FileNum, i.Err)
		return
	}

Use\n// of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be found in\n// the LICENSE file.\n\npackage pebble\n\nimport (\n\t\"runtime\"\n\t\"sync\"\n\t\"sync/atomic\"\n\n\t\"github.com/cockroachdb/crlib/crtime\"\n\t\"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/batchrepr\"\n\t\"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/base\"\n\t\"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/record\"\n)\n\n// commitQueue is a lock-free fixed-size single-producer, multi-consumer\n// queue. The single producer can enqueue (push) to the head, and consumers can\n// dequeue (pop) from the tail.\n//\n// It has the added feature that it nils out unused slots to avoid unnecessary\n// retention of objects.\ntype commitQueue struct {\n\t// headTail packs together a 32-bit head index and a 32-bit tail index. Both\n\t// are indexes into slots modulo len(slots)-1.\n\t//\n\t// tail = index of oldest data in queue\n\t// head = index of next slot to fill\n\t//\n\t// Slots in the range [tail, head) are owned by consumers.  A consumer\n\t// continues to own a slot outside this range until it nils the slot, at\n\t// which point ownership passes to the producer.\n\t//\n\t// The head index is stored in the most-significant bits so that we can\n\t// atomically add to it and the overflow is harmless.\n\theadTail atomic.Uint64\n\n\t// slots is a ring buffer of values stored in this queue. The size must be a\n\t// power of 2. A slot is in use until *both* the tail index has moved beyond\n\t// it and the slot value has been set to nil. The slot value is set to nil\n\t// atomically by the consumer and read atomically by the producer.\n\tslots [record.SyncConcurrency]atomic.Pointer[Batch]\n}\n\nconst dequeueBits = 32\n\nfunc (q *commitQueue) unpack(ptrs uint64) (head, tail uint32) {\n\tconst mask = 1\u003c\u003cdequeueBits - 1\n\thead = uint32((ptrs \u003e\u003e dequeueBits) \u0026 mask)\n\ttail = uint32(ptrs \u0026 mask)\n\treturn\n}\n\nfunc (q *commitQueue) pack(head, tail uint32) uint64 {\n\tconst mask = 1\u003c\u003cdequeueBits - 1\n\treturn (uint64(head) \u003c\u003c dequeueBits) |\n\t\tuint64(tail\u0026mask)\n}\n\nfunc (q *commitQueue) enqueue(b *Batch) {\n\tptrs := q.headTail.Load()\n\thead, tail := q.unpack(ptrs)\n\tif (tail+uint32(len(q.slots)))\u0026(1\u003c\u003cdequeueBits-1) == head {\n\t\t// Queue is full. This should never be reached because commitPipeline.commitQueueSem\n\t\t// limits the number of concurrent operations.\n\t\tpanic(\"pebble: not reached\")\n\t}\n\tslot := \u0026q.slots[head\u0026uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]\n\n\t// Check if the head slot has been released by dequeueApplied.\n\tfor slot.Load() != nil {\n\t\t// Another goroutine is still cleaning up the tail, so the queue is\n\t\t// actually still full. We spin because this should resolve itself\n\t\t// momentarily.\n\t\truntime.Gosched()\n\t}\n\n\t// The head slot is free, so we own it.\n\tslot.Store(b)\n\n\t// Increment head. This passes ownership of slot to dequeueApplied and acts as a\n\t// store barrier for writing the slot.\n\tq.headTail.Add(1 \u003c\u003c dequeueBits)\n}\n\n// dequeueApplied removes the earliest enqueued Batch, if it is applied.\n//\n// Returns nil if the commit queue is empty or the earliest Batch is not yet\n// applied.\nfunc (q *commitQueue) dequeueApplied() *Batch {\n\tfor {\n\t\tptrs := q.headTail.Load()\n\t\thead, tail := q.unpack(ptrs)\n\t\tif tail == head {\n\t\t\t// Queue is empty.\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tslot := \u0026q.slots[tail\u0026uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]\n\t\tb := slot.Load()\n\t\tif b == nil || !b.applied.Load() {\n\t\t\t// The batch is not ready to be dequeued, or another goroutine has\n\t\t\t// already dequeued it.\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Confirm head and tail (for our speculative check above) and increment\n\t\t// tail. If this succeeds, then we own the slot at tail.\n\t\tptrs2 := q.pack(head, tail+1)\n\t\tif q.headTail.CompareAndSwap(ptrs, ptrs2) {\n\t\t\t// We now own slot.\n\t\t\t//\n\t\t\t// Tell enqueue that we\u0027re done with this slot. Zeroing the slot is also\n\t\t\t// important so we don\u0027t leave behind references that could keep this object\n\t\t\t// live longer than necessary.\n\t\t\tslot.Store(nil)\n\t\t\t// At this point enqueue owns the slot.\n\t\t\treturn b\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// commitEnv contains the environment that a commitPipeline interacts\n// with. This allows fine-grained testing of commitPipeline behavior without\n// construction of an entire DB.\ntype commitEnv struct {\n\t// The next sequence number to give to a batch. Protected by\n\t// commitPipeline.mu.\n\tlogSeqNum *base.AtomicSeqNum\n\t// The visible sequence number at which reads should be performed. Ratcheted\n\t// upwards atomically as batches are applied to the memtable.\n\tvisibleSeqNum *base.AtomicSeqNum\n\n\t// Apply the batch to the specified memtable. Called concurrently.\n\tapply func(b *Batch, mem *memTable) error\n\t// Write the batch to the WAL. If wg != nil, the data will be persisted\n\t// asynchronously and done will be called on wg upon completion. If wg != nil\n\t// and err != nil, a failure to persist the WAL will populate *err.   "output_tail": "p *commitPipeline) AllocateSeqNum(\n\tcount int, prepare func(seqNum base.SeqNum), apply func(seqNum base.SeqNum),\n) {\n\t// This method is similar to Commit and prepare. Be careful about trying to\n\t// share additional code with those methods because Commit and prepare are\n\t// performance critical code paths.\n\n\tb := newBatch(nil)\n\tdefer func() { _ = b.Close() }()\n\n\t// Give the batch a count of 1 so that the log and visible sequence number\n\t// are incremented correctly.\n\tb.data = make([]byte, batchrepr.HeaderLen)\n\tb.setCount(uint32(count))\n\tb.commit.Add(1)\n\n\tp.commitQueueSem \u003c- struct{}{}\n\n\tp.mu.Lock()\n\n\t// Enqueue the batch in the pending queue. The steps are\n   147\t// conceptually:\n   148\t//\n   149\t//  1. Write the batch to the WAL and optionally sync the WAL\n   150\t//  2. In the absence of concurrency, performance is limited by how\n   154\t// fast a batch can be written (and synced) to the WAL and then added to the\n   155\t// memtable, both of which are outside the purview of the commit\n   156\t// pipeline. Note that writing to the WAL is\n   168\t// extremely fast, usually just a memory copy.    294	// Commit the specified batch, writing it to the WAL, optionally syncing the
   295	// WAL, and applying the batch to the memtable. Upon successful return the
   296	// batch's mutations will be visible for reading.
   297	// REQUIRES: noSyncWait => syncWAL
   298	func (p *commitPipeline) Commit(b *Batch, syncWAL bool, noSyncWait bool) error {
   299		if b.Empty() {
   300			return nil
   301		}
   302	
   303		commitStartTime := crtime.NowMono()
   304		// Acquire semaphores.
   305		p.commitQueueSem <- struct{}{}
   306		if syncWAL {
   307			p.logSyncQSem <- struct{}{}
   308		}
   309		b.commitStats.SemaphoreWaitDuration = commitStartTime.Elapsed()
   310	
   311		// Prepare the batch for committing: enqueuing the batch in the pending
   312		// queue, determining the batch sequence number and writing the data to the
   313		// WAL.
   314		//
   315		// NB: We set Batch.commitErr on error so that the batch won't be a candidate
   316		// for reuse. See Batch.release().
   317		mem, err := p.prepare(b, syncWAL, noSyncWait)
   318		if err != nil {
   319			b.db = nil // prevent batch reuse on error
   320			// NB: we are not doing <-p.commitQueueSem since the batch is still
   321			// sitting in the pending queue. We should consider fixing this by also
   322			// removing the batch from the pending queue.
   323			return err
   324		}
   325	
   326		// Apply the batch to the memtable.
   327		if err := p.env.apply(b, mem); err != nil {
   328			b.db = nil // prevent batch reuse on error
   329			// NB: we are not doing <-p.commitQueueSem since the batch is still
   330			// sitting in the pending queue. We should consider fixing this by also
   331			// removing the batch from the pending queue.
   332			return err
   333		}
   334	
   335		// Publish the batch sequence number.
   336		p.publish(b)
   337	
   338		<-p.commitQueueSem
   339	
   340		if !noSyncWait {
   341			// Already waited for commit, so look at the error.
   342			if b.commitErr != nil {
   343				b.db = nil // prevent batch reuse on error
   344				err = b.commitErr
   345			}
   346		}
   347		// Else noSyncWait. The LogWriter can be concurrently writing to
   348		// b.commitErr. We will read b.commitErr in Batch.SyncWait after the
   349		// LogWriter is done writing.
   350	
   351		b.commitStats.TotalDuration = commitStartTime.Elapsed()
   352	
   353		return err
   354	}
   355	
   356	// AllocateSeqNum allocates count sequence numbers, invokes the prepare
   357	// callback, then the apply callback, and then publishes the sequence
   358	// numbers. AllocateSeqNum does not write to the WAL or add entries to the
   359	// memtable. AllocateSeqNum can be used to sequence an operation such as
   360	// sstable ingestion within the commit pipeline. The prepare callback is
   361	// invoked with commitPipeline.mu held, but note that DB.mu is not held and
   362	// must be locked if necessary.
   363	func (p *commitPipeline) AllocateSeqNum(
   364		count int, prepare func(seqNum base.SeqNum), apply func(seqNum base.SeqNum),
   365	) {
   366		// This method is similar to Commit and prepare. Be careful about trying to
   367		// share additional code with those methods because Commit and prepare are
   368		// performance critical code paths.
   369	
   370		b := newBatch(nil)
   371		defer func() { _ = b.Close() }()
   372	
   373		// Give the batch a count of 1 so that the log and visible sequence number
   374		// are incremented correctly.
   375		b.data = make([]byte, batchrepr.HeaderLen)
   376		b.setCount(uint32(count))
   377		b.commit.Add(1)
   378	
   379		p.commitQueueSem <- struct{}{}
   380	
   381		p.mu.Lock()
   382	
   383		// Enqueue the batch in the pending queue. Note that while the pending queue
   384		// is lock-free, we want the order of batches to be the same as the sequence
   385		// number order.
   386		p.pending.enqueue(b)
   387	
   388		// Assign the batch a sequence number. Note that we use atomic operations
   389		// here to handle concurrent reads of logSeqNum. commitPipeline.mu provides
   390		// mutual exclusion for other goroutines writing to logSeqNum.
   391		logSeqNum := p.env.logSeqNum.Add(base.SeqNum(count)) - base.SeqNum(count)
   392		seqNum := logSeqNum
   393		if seqNum == 0 {
   394			// We can't use the value 0 for the global seqnum during ingestion, because
   395			// 0 indicates no global seqnum. So allocate one more seqnum.
   396			p.env.logSeqNum.Add(1)
   397			seqNum++
   398		}
   399		b.setSeqNum(seqNum)
   400	
   401		// Wait for any outstanding writes to the memtable to complete. This is
   402		// necessary for ingestion so that the check for memtable overlap can see any
   403		// writes that were sequenced before the ingestion. The spin loop is
   404		// unfortunate, but obviates the need for additional synchronization.
   405		for {
   406			visibleSeqNum := p.env.visibleSeqNum.Load()
   407			if visibleSeqNum == logSeqNum {
   408				break
   409			}
   410			runtime.Gosched()
   411		}
   412	
   413		// Invoke the prepare callback. Note the lack of error reporting. Even if the
   414		// callback internally fails, the sequence number needs to be published in
   415		// order to allow the commit pipeline to proceed.
   416		prepare(b.SeqNum())
   417	
   418		p.mu.Unlock()
   419	
   420		// Invoke the apply callback.
   421		apply(b.SeqNum())
   422	
   423		// Publish the sequence number.
   424		p.publish(b)
   425	
   426		<-p.commitQueueSem
   427	}
   428	
   429	func (p *commitPipeline) prepare(b *Batch, syncWAL bool, noSyncWait bool) (*memTable, error) {
   430		n := uint64(b.Count())
   431		if n == invalidBatchCount {
   432			return nil, ErrInvalidBatch
   433		}
   434		var syncWG *sync.WaitGroup
   435		var syncErr *error
   436		switch {
   437		case !syncWAL:
   438			// Only need to wait for the publish.
   439			b.commit.Add(1)
   440		// Remaining cases represent syncWAL=true.
   441		case noSyncWait:
   442			syncErr = &b.commitErr
   443			syncWG = &b.fsyncWait
   444			// Only need to wait synchronously for the publish. The user will
   445			// (asynchronously) wait on the batch's fsyncWait.
   446			b.commit.Add(1)
   447			b.fsyncWait.Add(1)
   448		case !noSyncWait:
   449			syncErr = &b.commitErr
   450			syncWG = &b.commit
   451			// Must wait for both the publish and the WAL fsync.
   452			b.commit.Add(2)
   453		}
   454	
   455		p.mu.Lock()
   456	
   457		// Enqueue the batch in the pending queue. Note that while the pending queue
   458		// is lock-free, we want the order of batches to be the same as the sequence
   459		// number order.
   460		p.pending.enqueue(b)
   461	
   462		// Assign the batch a sequence number. Note that we use atomic operations
   463		// here to handle concurrent reads of logSeqNum. commitPipeline.mu provides
   464		// mutual exclusion for other goroutines writing to logSeqNum.
   465		b.setSeqNum(p.env.logSeqNum.Add(base.SeqNum(n)) - base.SeqNum(n))
   466	
   467		// Write the data to the WAL.
   468		mem, err := p.env.write(b, syncWG, syncErr)
   469	
   470		p.mu.Unlock()
   471	
   472		return mem, err
   473	}
   474	
   475	func (p *commitPipeline) publish(b *Batch) {
   476		// Mark the batch as applied.
   477		b.applied.Store(true)
   478	
   479		// Loop dequeuing applied batches from the pending queue. If our batch was
   480		// the head of the pending queue we are guaranteed that either we'll publish
   481		// it or someone else will dequeueApplied and publish it. If our batch is not the
   482		// head of the queue then either we'll dequeueApplied applied batches and reach our
   483		// batch or there is an unapplied batch blocking us. When that unapplied
   484		// batch applies it will go through the same process and publish our batch
   485		// for us.
   486		for {
   487			t := p.pending.dequeueApplied()
   488			if t == nil {
   489				// Wait for another goroutine to publish us. We might also be waiting for
   490				// the WAL sync to finish.
   491				now := crtime.NowMono()
   492				b.commit.Wait()
   493				b.commitStats.CommitWaitDuration += now.Elapsed()
   494				break
   495			}
   496			if !t.applied.Load() {
   497				panic("not reached")
   498			}
   499	
   500			// We're responsible for publishing the sequence number for batch t, but
   501			// another concurrent goroutine might sneak in and publish the sequence
   502			// number for a subsequent batch. That's ok as all we're guaranteeing is
   503			// that the sequence number ratchets up.
   504			for {
   505				curSeqNum := p.env.visibleSeqNum.Load()
   506				newSeqNum := t.SeqNum() + base.SeqNum(t.Count())
   507				if newSeqNum <= curSeqNum {
   508					// t's sequence number has already been published.
   509					break
   510				}
   511				if p.env.visibleSeqNum.CompareAndSwap(curSeqNum, newSeqNum) {
   512					// We successfully published t's sequence number.
   513					break
   514				}
   515			}
   516	
   517			t.commit.Done()
   518		}
   519	}

const (
	syncConcurrencyBits = 12

	// SyncConcurrency is the maximum number of concurrent sync operations that
	// can be performed. Note that a sync operation is initiated either by a call
	// to SyncRecord or by a call to Close. Exported as this value also limits
	// the commit concurrency in commitPipeline.
	SyncConcurrency = 1 << syncConcurrencyBits
)

type syncSlot struct {
	wg  *sync.WaitGroup
	err *error
}

// syncQueue is a lock-free fixed-size single-producer, single-consumer
// queue. The single-producer can push to the head, and the single-consumer can
// pop multiple values from the tail. Popping calls Done() on each of the
// available *sync.WaitGroup elements.
type syncQueue struct {
	// headTail packs together a 32-bit head index and a 32-bit tail index. Both
	// are indexes into slots modulo len(slots)-1.
	//
	// tail = index of oldest data in queue
	// head = index of next slot to fill
	//
	// Slots in the range [tail, head) are owned by consumers.  A consumer
	// continues to own a slot outside this range until it nils the slot, at
	// which point ownership passes to the producer.
	//
	// The head index is stored in the most-significant bits so that we can
	// atomically add to it and the overflow is harmless.
	headTail atomic.Uint64

	// slots is a ring buffer of values stored in this queue. The size must be a
	// power of 2. A slot is in use until the tail index has moved beyond it.
	slots [SyncConcurrency]syncSlot

	// blocked is an atomic boolean which indicates whether syncing is currently
	// blocked or can proceed. It is used by the implementation of
	// min-sync-interval to block syncing until the min interval has passed.
	blocked atomic.Bool
}

const dequeueBits = 32

// unpack extracts the head and tail indices from a 64-bit unsigned integer.
func (q *syncQueue) unpack(ptrs uint64) (head, tail uint32) {
	const mask = 1<<dequeueBits - 1
	head = uint32((ptrs >> dequeueBits) & mask)
	tail = uint32(ptrs & mask)
	return head, tail
}

func (q *syncQueue) push(wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error) {
	ptrs := q.headTail.Load()
	head, tail := q.unpack(ptrs)
	if (tail+uint32(len(q.slots)))&(1<<dequeueBits-1) == head {
		panic("pebble: queue is full")
	}

	slot := &q.slots[head&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
	slot.wg = wg
	slot.err = err

	// Increment head. This passes ownership of slot to dequeue and acts as a
	// store barrier for writing the slot.
	q.headTail.Add(1 << dequeueBits)
}

func (q *syncQueue) setBlocked() {
	q.blocked.Store(true)
}

func (q *syncQueue) clearBlocked() {
	q.blocked.Store(false)
}

func (q *syncQueue) empty() bool {
	head, tail, _ := q.load()
	return head == tail
}

// load returns the head, tail of the queue for what should be synced to the
// caller. It can return a head, tail of zero if syncing is blocked due to
// min-sync-interval. It additionally returns the real length of this queue,
// regardless of whether syncing is blocked.
func (q *syncQueue) load() (head, tail, realLength uint32) {
	ptrs := q.headTail.Load()
	head, tail = q.unpack(ptrs)
	realLength = head - tail
	if q.blocked.Load() {
		return 0, 0, realLength
	}
	return head, tail, realLength
}

// REQUIRES: queueSemChan is non-nil.
func (q *syncQueue) pop(head, tail uint32, err error, queueSemChan chan struct{}) error {
	if tail == head {
		// Queue is empty.
		return nil
	}

	for ; tail != head; tail++ {
		slot := &q.slots[tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
		wg := slot.wg
		if wg == nil {
			return errors.Errorf("nil waiter at %d", errors.Safe(tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)))
		}
		*slot.err = err
		slot.wg = nil
		slot.err = nil
		// We need to bump the tail count before releasing the queueSemChan
		// semaphore as releasing the semaphore can cause a blocked goroutine to
		// acquire the semaphore and enqueue before we've "freed" space in the
		// queue.
		q.headTail.Add(1)
		wg.Done()
		// Is always non-nil in production, unless using wal package for WAL
		// failover.
		if queueSemChan != nil {
			<-queueSemChan
		}
	}

	return nil
}

// pendingSyncs abstracts out the handling of pending sync requests. In
// standalone mode the implementation is a thin wrapper around syncQueue. In
// the mode where the LogWriter can be subject to failover, there is no queue
// kept in the LogWriter and the signaling to those waiting for sync is
// handled in the wal package.
//
// To avoid heap allocations due to the use of this interface, the parameters
// and return values follow some strict rules:
//   - The PendingSync parameter can be reused by the caller after push returns.
//     The implementation should be a pointer backed by a struct that is already
//     heap allocated, which the caller can reuse for the next push call.
//   - The pendingSyncSnapshot return value must be backed by the pendingSyncs
//     implementation, so calling snapshotForPop again will cause the previous
//     snapshot to be overwritten.
type pendingSyncs interface {
	push(PendingSync)
	setBlocked()
	clearBlocked()
	empty() bool
	snapshotForPop() pendingSyncsSnapshot
	pop(snap pendingSyncsSnapshot, err error) error
}

type pendingSyncsSnapshot interface {
	empty() bool
}

// PendingSync abstracts the sync specification for a record queued on the
// LogWriter. The only implementations are provided in this package since
// syncRequested is not exported.
type PendingSync interface {
	syncRequested() bool
}

// The implementation of pendingSyncs in standalone mode.
type pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue struct {
	syncQueue
	syncQueueLen    *base.GaugeSampleMetric
	snapshotBacking syncQueueSnapshot
	// See the comment for LogWriterConfig.QueueSemChan.
	queueSemChan chan struct{}
}

var _ pendingSyncs = &pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue{}

func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) push(ps PendingSync) {
	ps2 := ps.(*pendingSyncForSyncQueue)
	q.syncQueue.push(ps2.wg, ps2.err)
}

func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) snapshotForPop() pendingSyncsSnapshot {
	head, tail, realLength := q.syncQueue.load()
	q.snapshotBacking = syncQueueSnapshot{
		head: head,
		tail: tail,
	}
	q.syncQueueLen.AddSample(int64(realLength))
	return &q.snapshotBacking
}

func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) pop(snap pendingSyncsSnapshot, err error) error {
	s := snap.(*syncQueueSnapshot)
	return q.syncQueue.pop(s.head, s.tail, err, q.queueSemChan)
}

// The implementation of pendingSyncsSnapshot in standalone mode.
type syncQueueSnapshot struct {
	head, tail uint32
}

func (s *syncQueueSnapshot) empty() bool {
	return s.head == s.tail
}

// The implementation of pendingSync in standalone mode.
type pendingSyncForSyncQueue struct {
	wg  *sync.WaitGroup
	err *error
}

func (ps *pendingSyncForSyncQueue) syncRequested() bool {
	return ps.wg != nil
}

// The implementation of pendingSyncs in failover mode.
type pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex struct {
	// The highest "index" queued that is requesting a sync. Initialized
	// to NoSyncIndex, and reset to NoSyncIndex after the sync.
	index           atomic.Int64
	snapshotBacking PendingSyncIndex

   853	func (d *DB) commitApply(b *Batch, mem *memTable) error {
   854		if b.flushable != nil {
   855			// This is a large batch which was already added to the immutable queue.
   856			return nil
   857		}
   858		err := mem.apply(b, b.SeqNum())
   859		if err != nil {
   860			return err
   861		}
   862	
   863		// If the batch contains range tombstones and the database is configured
   864		// to flush range deletions, schedule a delayed flush so that disk space
   865		// may be reclaimed without additional writes or an explicit flush.
   866		if b.countRangeDels > 0 && d.opts.FlushDelayDeleteRange > 0 {
   867			d.mu.Lock()
   868			d.maybeScheduleDelayedFlush(mem, d.opts.FlushDelayDeleteRange)
   869			d.mu.Unlock()
   870		}
   871	
   872		// If the batch contains range keys and the database is configured to flush
   873		// range keys, schedule a delayed flush so that the range keys are cleared
   874		// from the memtable.
   875		if b.countRangeKeys > 0 && d.opts.FlushDelayRangeKey > 0 {
   876			d.mu.Lock()
   877			d.maybeScheduleDelayedFlush(mem, d.opts.FlushDelayRangeKey)
   878			d.mu.Unlock()
   879		}
   880	
   881		if mem.writerUnref() {
   882			d.mu.Lock()
   883			d.maybeScheduleFlush()
   884			d.mu.Unlock()
   885		}
   886		return nil
   887	}
   888	
   889	func (d *DB) commitWrite(b *Batch, syncWG *sync.WaitGroup, syncErr *error) (*memTable, error) {
   890		var size int64
   891		repr := b.Repr()
   892	
   893		if b.flushable != nil {
   894			// We have a large batch. Such batches are special in that they don't get
   895			// added to the memtable, and are instead inserted into the queue of
   896			// memtables. The call to makeRoomForWrite with this batch will force the
   897			// current memtable to be flushed. We want the large batch to be part of
   898			// the same log, so we add it to the WAL here, rather than after the call
   899			// to makeRoomForWrite().
   900			//
   901			// Set the sequence number since it was not set to the correct value earlier
   902			// (see comment in newFlushableBatch()).
   903			b.flushable.setSeqNum(b.SeqNum())
   904			if !d.opts.DisableWAL {
   905				var err error
   906				size, err = d.mu.log.writer.WriteRecord(repr, wal.SyncOptions{Done: syncWG, Err: syncErr}, b)
   907				if err != nil {
   908					panic(err)
   909				}
   910			}
   911		}
   912	
   913		var err error
   914		// Grab a reference to the memtable. We don't hold DB.mu, but we do hold
   915		// d.commit.mu. It's okay for readers of d.mu.mem.mutable to only hold one of
   916		// d.commit.mu or d.mu, because memtable rotations require holding both.
   917		mem := d.mu.mem.mutable
   918		// Batches which contain keys of kind InternalKeyKindIngestSST or InternalKeyKindIngestSSTWithBlobs will
   919		// never be applied to the memtable, so we don't need to make room for
   920		// write.
   921		if !b.ingestedSSTBatch {
   922			// Flushable batches will require a rotation of the memtable regardless,
   923			// so only attempt an optimistic reservation of space in the current
   924			// memtable if this batch is not a large flushable batch.
   925			if b.flushable == nil {
   926				err = d.mu.mem.mutable.prepare(b)
   927			}
   928			if b.flushable != nil || err == arenaskl.ErrArenaFull {
   929				// Slow path.
   930				// We need to acquire DB.mu and rotate the memtable.
   931				func() {
   932					d.mu.Lock()
   933					defer d.mu.Unlock()
   934					err = d.makeRoomForWrite(b)
   935					mem = d.mu.mem.mutable
   936				}()
   937			}
   938		}
   939		if err != nil {
   940			return nil, err
   941		}
   942		if d.opts.DisableWAL {
   943			return mem, nil
   944		}
   945		d.logBytesIn.Add(uint64(len(repr)))
   946	
   947		if b.flushable == nil {
   948			size, err = d.mu.log.writer.WriteRecord(repr, wal.SyncOptions{Done: syncWG, Err: syncErr}, b)
   949			if err != nil {
   950				panic(err)
   951			}
   952		}
   953	
   954		d.logSize.Store(uint64(size))
   955		return mem, err
   956	}

   973	func (i *iterAlloc) maybeAssertZeroed() {

   977				panic(err)
   978			}

   981	
   982	// assertZeroed asserts that v is a "zeroed" value. A "zeroed" value is a value
   983	// that is:
   984	//   - the Go zero value for its type, or
   985	//   - a pointer to a "zeroed" value, or
   986	//   - a slice of len()=0, with any values in the backing array being "zeroed
   987	//     values", or
   988	//   - a struct with all fields being "zeroed" values,
   989	//   - a struct field explicitly marked with a "invariants:reused" tag.
   990	func assertZeroed(v reflect.Value) error {
   991		if v.IsZero() {
   992			return nil
   993		}
   994		typ := v.Type()
   995		switch typ.Kind() {
   996		case reflect.Pointer:
   997			return assertZeroed(v.Elem())
   998		case reflect.Slice:
   999			if v.Len() > 0 {

// TODO(sumeer): write a high-level comment describing the approach.

// StableIdentifierFilename is the name of the file within a WAL directory that
// stores a stable ID identifying that directory. This is used to detect when
// the wrong disk has been mounted at the expected path during recovery.
const StableIdentifierFilename = "stable_identifier"

// Dir is used for storing log files.
type Dir struct {
	Lock    *base.DirLock
	FS      vfs.FS
	Dirname string
	// ID is a stable ID that uniquely identifies the directory. This
	// identifier is persisted both in the OPTIONS file in the primary directory
	// and in a file (stable_identifier) within the secondary directory to detect
	// incorrectness/corruptions (e.g. when the wrong disk has been mounted at
	// the expected path during recovery).
	ID string
}

// NumWAL is the number of the virtual WAL. It can map to one or more physical
// log files. In standalone mode, it will map to exactly one log file. In
// failover mode, it can map to many log files, which are totally ordered
// (using a dense logNameIndex).
//
// In general, WAL refers to the virtual WAL, and file refers to a log file.
// The Pebble MANIFEST only knows about virtual WALs and assigns numbers to
// them. Additional mapping to one or more files happens in this package. If a
// WAL maps to multiple files, the source of truth regarding that mapping is
// the contents of the directories.
type NumWAL base.DiskFileNum

// String implements fmt.Stringer.
func (s NumWAL) String() string { return base.DiskFileNum(s).String() }

// LogNameIndex numbers log files within a WAL.
type LogNameIndex uint32

// String implements fmt.Stringer.
func (li LogNameIndex) String() string {
	return fmt.Sprintf("%03d", li)
}

// makeLogFilename makes a log filename.
func makeLogFilename(wn NumWAL, index LogNameIndex) string {
	if index == 0 {
		// Use a backward compatible name, for simplicity.
		return fmt.Sprintf("%s.log", base.DiskFileNum(wn).String())
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("%s-%s.log", base.DiskFileNum(wn).String(), index)
}

// ParseLogFilename takes a base filename and parses it into its constituent
// NumWAL and LogNameIndex. If the filename is not a log file, it returns false
// for the final return value.
func ParseLogFilename(name string) (NumWAL, LogNameIndex, bool) {
	i := strings.IndexByte(name, '.')
	if i < 0 || name[i:] != ".log" {
		return 0, 0, false
	}
	j := strings.IndexByte(name[:i], '-')
	if j < 0 {
		dfn, ok := base.ParseDiskFileNum(name[:i])
		if !ok {
			// We've considered returning an error for filenames that end in
			// '.log' but fail to parse correctly. We decided against it because
			// the '.log' suffix is used by Cockroach's daignostics log files.
			// It's conceivable that some of these found their way into a data
			// directory, and erroring would cause an issue for an existing
			// Cockroach deployment.
			return 0, 0, false
		}
		return NumWAL(dfn), 0, true
	}
	dfn, ok := base.ParseDiskFileNum(name[:j])
	if !ok {
		return 0, 0, false
	}
	li, err := strconv.ParseUint(name[j+1:i], 10, 64)
	if err != nil {
		return 0, 0, false
	}
	return NumWAL(dfn), LogNameIndex(li), true
}

// Options provides configuration for the Manager.
type Options struct {
	// Primary dir for storing WAL files. It must already be created and synced
	// up to the root.
	Primary Dir
	// Secondary is used for failover. Optional. It must already be created and
	// synced up to the root.
	Secondary Dir

	// MinUnflushedLogNum is the smallest WAL number corresponding to
	// mutations that have not been flushed to a sstable.
	MinUnflushedWALNum NumWAL

	// Recycling configuration. Only files in the primary dir are recycled.

	// MaxNumRecyclableLogs is the maximum number of log files to maintain for
	// recycling.
	MaxNumRecyclableLogs int

	// Configuration for calling vfs.NewSyncingFile.

	// NoSyncOnClose is documented in SyncingFileOptions.
	NoSyncOnClose bool
	// BytesPerSync is documented in SyncingFileOptions.
	BytesPerSync int
	// PreallocateSize is documented in SyncingFileOptions.
	PreallocateSize func() int

	// MinSyncInterval is documented in Options.WALMinSyncInterval.
	MinSyncInterval func() time.Duration

	// WAL file operation latency histograms
	PrimaryFileOpHistogram   record.WALFileOpHistogram
	SecondaryFileOpHistogram record.WALFileOpHistogram
	// QueueSemChan is the channel to pop from when popping from queued records
	// that have requested a sync. It's original purpose was to function as a
	// semaphore that prevents the record.LogWriter.flusher.syncQueue from
	// overflowing (which will cause a panic). It is still useful in that role
	// when the WALManager is configured in standalone mode. In failover mode
	// there is no syncQueue, so the pushback into the commit pipeline is
	// unnecessary, but possibly harmless.
	QueueSemChan chan struct{}

	// Logger for logging.
	Logger base.Logger

	// EventListener is called on events, like log file creation.
	EventListener EventListener

	FailoverOptions
	// FailoverWriteAndSyncLatency is only populated when WAL failover is
	// configured.
	FailoverWriteAndSyncLatency prometheus.Histogram

	// WriteWALSyncOffsets determines whether to write WAL sync chunk offsets.
	// The format major version can change (ratchet) at runtime, so this must be
	// a function rather than a static bool to ensure we use the latest format version.
	// It is plumbed down from wal.Options to record.newLogWriter.
	WriteWALSyncOffsets func() bool
}

// Init constructs and initializes a WAL manager from the provided options and
// the set of initial logs. The initial parameter must contain all WAL files
// that have not been deleted. It is only used to queue these up for deletion
// at the appropriate time (i.e. when their numbers are less than minUnflushedNum)
// and to ensure that they won't be recycled.
func Init(o Options, initial Logs) (Manager, error) {
	var m Manager
	if o.Secondary == (Dir{}) {
		m = new(StandaloneManager)
	} else {
		m = new(failoverManager)
	}
	if err := m.init(o, initial); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return m, nil
}

// Dirs returns the primary Dir and the secondary if provided.
func (o *Options) Dirs() []Dir {
	if o.Secondary == (Dir{}) {
		return []Dir{o.Primary}
	}
	return []Dir{o.Primary, o.Secondary}
}

// FailoverOptions are options that are specific to failover mode.
type FailoverOptions struct {
	// PrimaryDirProbeInterval is the interval for probing the primary dir, when
	// the WAL is being written to the secondary, to decide when to fail back.
	PrimaryDirProbeInterval time.Duration
	// HealthyProbeLatencyThreshold is the latency threshold to declare that the
	// primary is healthy again.
	HealthyProbeLatencyThreshold time.Duration
	// HealthyInterval is the time interval over which the probes have to be
	// healthy. That is, we look at probe history of length
	// HealthyInterval/PrimaryDirProbeInterval.
	HealthyInterval time.Duration

	// UnhealthySamplingInterval is the interval for sampling ongoing calls and
	// errors in the latest LogWriter.
	UnhealthySamplingInterval time.Duration
	// UnhealthyOperationLatencyThreshold is the latency threshold that is
	// considered unhealthy, for operations done by a LogWriter. The second return
	// value indicates whether we should consider failover at all. If the second
	// return value is false, failover is disabled.
	UnhealthyOperationLatencyThreshold func() (time.Duration, bool)

	// ElevatedWriteStallThresholdLag is the duration for which an elevated
	// threshold should continue after a switch back to the primary dir. This is
	// because we may have accumulated many unflushed memtables and flushing
	// them can take some time. Maybe set to 60s.
	ElevatedWriteStallThresholdLag time.Duration

	// timeSource is only non-nil for tests.
	timeSource

	monitorIterationForTesting chan<- struct{}
	proberIterationForTesting  chan<- struct{}
	monitorStateForTesting     func(numSwitches int, ongoingLatencyAtSwitch time.Duration)
	logWriterCreatedForTesting chan<- struct{}
}

	if o.HealthyProbeLatencyThreshold == 0 {
		o.HealthyProbeLatencyThreshold = 25 * time.Millisecond
	}
	if o.HealthyInterval == 0 {

// SyncOptions has non-nil Done and Err when fsync is requested, else both are

}

// Writer writes to a virtual WAL. A Writer in standalone mode maps to a
// single record.LogWriter. In failover mode, it can failover across multiple
// physical log files.
type Writer interface {
	// WriteRecord writes a complete record. The record is asynchronously
	// persisted to the underlying writer. If SyncOptions.Done != nil, the wait
	// group will be notified when durability is guaranteed or an error has
	// occurred (set in SyncOptions.Err). External synchronisation provided by
	// commitPipeline.mu guarantees that WriteRecord calls are serialized.
	//
	// The logicalOffset is the logical size of the WAL after this record is
	// written. If the WAL corresponds to a single log file, this is the offset
	// in that log file.
	//
	// Some Writer implementations may continue to read p after WriteRecord
	// returns. This is an obstacle to reusing p's memory. If the caller would
	// like to reuse p's memory, the caller may pass a non-nil [RefCount].  If
	// the Writer will retain p, it will invoke the [RefCount] before returning.
	// When it's finished, it will invoke [RefCount.Unref] to release its
	// reference.
	WriteRecord(p []byte, opts SyncOptions, ref RefCount) (logicalOffset int64, err error)
	// Close the writer.
	Close() (logicalOffset int64, err error)
	// Metrics must be called after Close. The callee will no longer modify the
	// returned LogWriterMetrics.
	Metrics() record.LogWriterMetrics
}

// RefCount is a reference count associated with a record passed to
// [Writer.WriteRecord]. See the comment on WriteRecord.
type RefCount interface {
	// Ref increments the reference count.
	Ref()
	// Unref increments the reference count.
	Unref()
}

   741	
   742	// doneSyncCallback is the record.ExternalSyncQueueCallback called by
   743	// record.LogWriter.
   744	//
   745	// recordQueue is popped from only when some work requests a sync (and
   746	// successfully syncs). In the worst case, if no syncs are requested, we could
   747	// queue all the records needed to fill up a memtable in the recordQueue. This
   748	// can have two negative effects: (a) in the case of failover, we need to
   749	// replay all the data in the current mutable memtable, which takes more time,
   750	// (b) the memory usage is proportional to the size of the memtable. We ignore
   751	// these negatives since, (a) users like CockroachDB regularly sync, and (b)
   752	// the default memtable size is only 64MB.
   753	func (ww *failoverWriter) doneSyncCallback(doneSync record.PendingSyncIndex, err error) {
   754		if err != nil {
   755			// Don't pop anything since we can retry after switching to a new
   756			// LogWriter.
   757			return
   758		}
   759		// NB: harmless after Close returns since numSyncsPopped will be 0.
   760		numSyncsPopped := ww.q.pop(uint32(doneSync.Index), err)
   761		if ww.opts.queueSemChan != nil {
   762			for i := 0; i < numSyncsPopped; i++ {
   763				<-ww.opts.queueSemChan
   764			}
   765		}
   766	}

   769	func (ww *failoverWriter) ongoingLatencyOrErrorForCurDir() (time.Duration, error) {
   770		r := ww.recorderForCurDir()
   771		if r == nil {
   772			return 0, nil
   773		}
   774		return r.ongoingLatencyOrError()
   775	}

   392	// BatchCommitStats exposes stats related to committing a batch.
   393	//
   394	// NB: there is no Pebble internal tracing (using LoggerAndTracer) of slow
   395	// batch commits. The caller can use these stats to do their own tracing as
   396	// needed.
   397	type BatchCommitStats struct {
   398		// TotalDuration is the time spent in DB.{Apply,ApplyNoSyncWait} or
   399		// Batch.Commit, plus the time waiting in Batch.SyncWait. If there is a gap
   400		// between calling ApplyNoSyncWait and calling SyncWait, that gap could
   401		// include some duration in which real work was being done for the commit
   402		// and will not be included here. This missing time is considered acceptable
   403		// since the goal of these stats is to understand user-facing latency.
   404		//
   405		// TotalDuration includes time spent in various queues both inside Pebble
   406		// and outside Pebble (I/O queues, goroutine scheduler queue, mutex wait
   407		// etc.). For some of these queues (which we consider important) the wait
   408		// times are included below -- these expose low-level implementation detail
   409		// and are meant for expert diagnosis and subject to change. There may be
   410		// unaccounted time after subtracting those values from TotalDuration.
   411		TotalDuration time.Duration
   412		// SemaphoreWaitDuration is the wait time for semaphores in
   413		// commitPipeline.Commit.
   414		SemaphoreWaitDuration time.Duration
   415		// WALQueueWaitDuration is the wait time for allocating memory blocks in the
   416		// LogWriter (due to the LogWriter not writing fast enough). At the moment
   417		// this is duration is always zero because a single WAL will allow
   418		// allocating memory blocks up to the entire memtable size. In the future,
   419		// we may pipeline WALs and bound the WAL queued blocks separately, so this
   420		// field is preserved for that possibility.
   421		WALQueueWaitDuration time.Duration
   422		// MemTableWriteStallDuration is the wait caused by a write stall due to too
   423		// many memtables (due to not flushing fast enough).
   424		MemTableWriteStallDuration time.Duration
   425		// L0ReadAmpWriteStallDuration is the wait caused by a write stall due to
   426		// high read amplification in L0 (due to not compacting fast enough out of
   427		// L0).
   428		L0ReadAmpWriteStallDuration time.Duration
   429		// WALRotationDuration is the wait time for WAL rotation, which includes
   430		// syncing and closing the old WAL and creating (or reusing) a new one.
   431		WALRotationDuration time.Duration
   432		// CommitWaitDuration is the wait for publishing the seqnum plus the
   433		// duration for the WAL sync (if requested). The former should be tiny and
   434		// one can assume that this is all due to the WAL sync.
   435		CommitWaitDuration time.Duration
   436	}

  1665	func (b *Batch) setSeqNum(seqNum base.SeqNum) {
  1666		batchrepr.SetSeqNum(b.data, seqNum)
  1667	}
  1668	
  1669	// SeqNum returns the batch sequence number which is applied to the first
  1670	// record in the batch. The sequence number is incremented for each subsequent
  1671	// record. It returns zero if the batch is empty.
  1672	func (b *Batch) SeqNum() base.SeqNum {
  1673		if len(b.data) == 0 {
  1674			b.init(batchrepr.HeaderLen)
  1675		}
  1676		return batchrepr.ReadSeqNum(b.data)
  1677	}
  1678	
  1679	func (b *Batch) setCount(v uint32) {
  1680		b.count = uint64(v)
  1681	}
  1682	
  1683	// Count returns the count of memtable-modifying operations in this batch. All
  1684	// operations with the except of LogData increment this count. For IngestSSTs,
  1685	// count is only used to indicate the number of SSTs ingested in the record, the
  1686	// batch isn't applied to the memtable.
  1687	func (b *Batch) Count() uint32 {
  1688		if b.count > math.MaxUint32 {
  1689			panic(batchrepr.ErrInvalidBatch)
  1690		}
  1691		return uint32(b.count)
  1692	}
  1693	
  1694	// Reader returns a batchrepr.Reader for the current batch contents. If the
  1695	// batch is mutated, the new entries will not be visible to the reader.
  1696	func (b *Batch) Reader() batchrepr.Reader {
  1697		if len(b.data) == 0 {
  1698			b.init(batchrepr.HeaderLen)
  1699		}
  1700		return batchrepr.Read(b.data)
  1701	}
  1702	
  1703	// SyncWait is to be used in conjunction with DB.ApplyNoSyncWait.
  1704	func (b *Batch) SyncWait() error {
  1705		now := crtime.NowMono()
  1706		b.fsyncWait.Wait()
  1707		if b.commitErr != nil {
  1708			b.db = nil // prevent batch reuse on error
  1709		}
  1710		waitDuration := now.Elapsed()
  1711		b.commitStats.CommitWaitDuration += waitDuration
  1712		b.commitStats.TotalDuration += waitDuration
  1713		return b.commitErr
  1714	}
  1715	
  1716	// CommitStats returns stats related to committing the batch. Should be called
  1717	// after Batch.Commit, DB.Apply. If DB.ApplyNoSyncWait is used, should be
  1718	// called after Batch.SyncWait.
  1719	func (b *Batch) CommitStats() BatchCommitStats {
  1720		return b.commitStats
  1721	}

   743	// It is safe to modify the contents of the arguments after RangeKeyDelete

   747		_ = b.RangeKeyDelete(start, end, opts)

   754	
   755	// Apply the operations contained in the batch to the DB. If the batch is large
   756	// the contents of the batch may be retained by the database. If that occurs
   757	// the batch contents will be cleared preventing the caller from attempting to
   758	// reuse them.
   759	//
   760	// It is safe to modify the contents of the arguments after Apply returns.
   761	//
   762	// Apply returns ErrInvalidBatch if the provided batch is invalid in any way.
   763	func (d *DB) Apply(batch *Batch, opts *WriteOptions) error {
   764		return d.applyInternal(batch, opts, false)
   765	}
   766	
   767	// ApplyNoSyncWait must only be used when opts.Sync is true and the caller
   768	// does not want to wait for the WAL fsync to happen. The method will return
   769	// once the mutation is applied to the memtable and is visible (note that a
   770	// mutation is visible before the WAL sync even in the wait case, so we have
   771	// not weakened the durability semantics). The caller must call Batch.SyncWait
   772	// to wait for the WAL fsync. The caller must not Close the batch without
   773	// first calling Batch.SyncWait.
   774	//
   775	// RECOMMENDATION: Prefer using Apply unless you really understand why you
   776	// need ApplyNoSyncWait.
   777	// EXPERIMENTAL: API/feature subject to change. Do not yet use outside
   778	// CockroachDB.
   779	func (d *DB) ApplyNoSyncWait(batch *Batch, opts *WriteOptions) error {
   780		if !opts.Sync {
   781			return errors.Errorf("cannot request asynchonous apply when WriteOptions.Sync is false")
   782		}
   783		return d.applyInternal(batch, opts, true)
   784	}
   785	
   786	// REQUIRES: noSyncWait => opts.Sync
   787	func (d *DB) applyInternal(batch *Batch, opts *WriteOptions, noSyncWait bool) error {
   788		if err := d.closed.Load(); err != nil {
   789			panic(err)
   790		}
   791		if batch.committing {
   792			panic("pebble: batch already committing")
   793		}
   794		if batch.applied.Load() {
   795			panic("pebble: batch already applied")
   796		}
   797		if d.opts.ReadOnly {
   798			return ErrReadOnly
   799		}
   800		if batch.db != nil && batch.db != d {
   801			panic(errors.AssertionFailedf("pebble: batch db mismatch: %p != %p", errors.Safe(batch.db), errors.Safe(d)))
   802		}
   803	
   804		sync := opts.GetSync()
   805		if sync && d.opts.DisableWAL {
   806			return errors.New("pebble: WAL disabled")
   807		}
   808	
   809		if fmv := d.FormatMajorVersion(); fmv < batch.minimumFormatMajorVersion {
   810			panic(errors.AssertionFailedf(
   811				"pebble: batch requires at least format major version %d (current: %d)",
   812				batch.minimumFormatMajorVersion, fmv,
   813			))
   814		}
   815	
   816		if batch.countRangeKeys > 0 {
   817			if d.split == nil {
   818				return errNoSplit
   819			}
   820		}
   821		batch.committing = true
   822	
   823		if batch.db == nil {
   824			if err := batch.refreshMemTableSize(); err != nil {
   825				return err
   826			}
   827		}
   828		if batch.memTableSize >= d.largeBatchThreshold {
   829			var err error
   830			batch.flushable, err = newFlushableBatch(batch, d.opts.Comparer)
   831			if err != nil {
   832				return err
   833			}
   834		}
   835		if err := d.commit.Commit(batch, sync, noSyncWait); err != nil {
   836			// There isn't much we can do on an error here. The commit pipeline will be
   837			// horked at this point.
   838			d.opts.Logger.Fatalf("pebble: fatal commit error: %v", err)
   839		}
   840		// If this is a large batch, we need to clear the batch contents as the
   841		// flushable batch may still be present in the flushables queue.
   842		//
   843		// TODO(peter): Currently large batches are written to the WAL. We could
   844		// skip the WAL write and instead wait for the large batch to be flushed to
   845		// an sstable. For a 100 MB batch, this might actually be faster. For a 1
   846		// GB batch this is almost certainly faster.
   847		if batch.flushable != nil {
   848			batch.data = nil
   849		}
   850		return nil
   851	}
   852

   854		if b.flushable != nil {
   855			// This is a large batch which was already added to the immutable queue.

It\n    38\t// is not lock-free, but most operations only need mu.RLock. It needs a mutex\n    39\t// to grow the size, since there is no upper bound on the number of queued\n    40\t// records (which are all the records that are not synced, and will need to be\n    41\t// written again in case of failover). This is not the usual\n    54\t\t// kind of queue consumer since they already know the index that they are\n    55\t\t// popping exists, hence don\u0027t need to look at head.\n    56\t\t//\n    57\t\t// Producer: atomically reads tail in push. Writes to head.\n    58\t\t//\n    59\t\t// Based on the above we only need tail to be atomic. However, the producer\n    60\t\t// also populates entries in buffer, whose values need to be seen by the\n    61\t\t// consumers when doing a pop, which means they need to synchronize using a\n    62\t\t// release and acquire memory barrier pair, where the push does the release\n    63\t\t// and the pop does the acquire.    268	// NoSyncIndex is the value of PendingSyncIndex when a sync is not requested.
   269	const NoSyncIndex = -1

   271	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) init(

   275		si.externalSyncQueueCallback = externalSyncQueueCallback
   276	}
   277	
   278	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) push(ps PendingSync) {
   279		ps2 := ps.(*PendingSyncIndex)
   280		si.index.Store(ps2.Index)
   281	}
   282

   291	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) empty() bool {
   292		return si.load() == NoSyncIndex
   293	}
   294	
   295	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) snapshotForPop() pendingSyncsSnapshot {
   296		si.snapshotBacking = PendingSyncIndex{Index: si.load()}
   297		return &si.snapshotBacking
   298	}
   299	
   300	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) load() int64 {
   301		index := si.index.Load()
   302		if index != NoSyncIndex && si.blocked.Load() {
   303			index = NoSyncIndex
   304		}
   305		return index
   306	}
   307	
   308	func (si *pendingSyncsWithHighestSyncIndex) pop(snap pendingSyncsSnapshot, err error) error {
   309		index := snap.(*PendingSyncIndex)
   310		if index.Index == NoSyncIndex {
   311			return nil
   312		}
   313		// Set to NoSyncIndex if a higher index has not queued.
   314		si.index.CompareAndSwap(index.Index, NoSyncIndex)
   315		si.externalSyncQueueCallback(*index, err)
   316		return nil
   317	}
   318	
   319	// PendingSyncIndex implements both pendingSyncsSnapshot and PendingSync.
   320	type PendingSyncIndex struct {
   321		// Index is some state meaningful to the user of LogWriter. The LogWriter
   322		// itself only examines whether Index is equal to NoSyncIndex.
   323		Index int64
   324	}
   325	
   326	func (s *PendingSyncIndex) empty() bool {
   327		return s.Index == NoSyncIndex
   328	}
   329	
   330	func (s *PendingSyncIndex) syncRequested() bool {
   331		return s.Index != NoSyncIndex
   332	}

   334	// flusherCond is a specialized condition variable that allows its condition to
   335	// change and readiness be signalled without holding its associated mutex. In
   336	// particular, when a waiter is added to syncQueue atomically, this condition
   337	// variable can be signalled without holding flusher.Mutex.
   338	type flusherCond struct {
   339		mu   *sync.Mutex
   340		q    pendingSyncs
   341		cond sync.Cond
   342	}
   343	
   344	func (c *flusherCond) init(mu *sync.Mutex, q pendingSyncs) {
   345		c.mu = mu
   346		c.q = q
   347		// Yes, this is a bit circular, but that is intentional. flusherCond.cond.L
   348		// points flusherCond so that when cond.L.Unlock is called flusherCond.Unlock
   349		// will be called and we can check the !syncQueue.empty() condition.
   350		c.cond.L = c
   351	}

   354		// Pass-through to the cond var.

   356	}

   940	// firstError returns the first non-nil error of err0 and err1, or nil if both

   949	// WriteRecord writes a complete record. Returns the offset just past the end
   950	// of the record.
   951	// External synchronisation provided by commitPipeline.mu.
   952	func (w *LogWriter) WriteRecord(p []byte) (int64, error) {
   953		logSize, err := w.SyncRecord(p, nil, nil)
   954		return logSize, err
   955	}
   956	
   957	// SyncRecord writes a complete record. If wg != nil the record will be
   958	// asynchronously persisted to the underlying writer and done will be called on
   959	// the wait group upon completion. Returns the offset just past the end of the
   960	// record.
   961	// External synchronisation provided by commitPipeline.mu.
   962	func (w *LogWriter) SyncRecord(
   963		p []byte, wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error,
   964	) (logSize int64, err2 error) {
   965		w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking = pendingSyncForSyncQueue{
   966			wg:  wg,
   967			err: err,
   968		}
   969		return w.SyncRecordGeneralized(p, &w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking)
   970	}
   971	
   972	// SyncRecordGeneralized is a version of SyncRecord that accepts a
   973	// PendingSync.
   974	func (w *LogWriter) SyncRecordGeneralized(p []byte, ps PendingSync) (logSize int64, err2 error) {
   975		if w.err != nil {
   976			return -1, w.err
   977		}
   978	
   979		// The `i == 0` condition ensures we handle empty records. Such records can
   980		// possibly be generated for VersionEdits stored in the MANIFEST. While the
   981		// MANIFEST is currently written using Writer, it is good to support the same
   982		// semantics with LogWriter.
   983		for i := 0; i == 0 || len(p) > 0; i++ {
   984			p = w.emitFragment(i, p)
   985		}
   986	
   987		if ps.syncRequested() {
   988			// If we've been asked to persist the record, add the WaitGroup to the sync
   989			// queue and signal the flushLoop. Note that flushLoop will write partial
   990			// blocks to the file if syncing has been requested. The contract is that
   991			// any record written to the LogWriter to this point will be flushed to the
   992			// OS and synced to disk.
   993			f := &w.flusher
   994			f.pendingSyncs.push(ps)
   995			f.ready.Signal()
   996		}
   997	
   998		offset := w.blockNum*blockSize + int64(w.block.written.Load())
   999		// Note that we don't return w.err here as a concurrent call to Close would
  1000		// race with our read. That's ok because the only error we could be seeing is
  1001		// one to syncing for which the caller can receive notification of by passing
  1002		// in a non-nil err argument.
  1003		return offset, nil
  1004	}

  1007	// External synchronisation provided by commitPipeline.mu.
  1008	func (w *LogWriter) Size() int64 {
  1009		return w.blockNum*blockSize + int64(w.block.written.Load())
  1010	}

type standaloneWriter struct {
	m *StandaloneManager
	w *record.LogWriter
}

var _ Writer = &standaloneWriter{}

// WriteRecord implements Writer.
func (w *standaloneWriter) WriteRecord(
	p []byte, opts SyncOptions, _ RefCount,
) (logicalOffset int64, err error) {
	return w.w.SyncRecord(p, opts.Done, opts.Err)
}

// Close implements Writer.
func (w *standaloneWriter) Close() (logicalOffset int64, err error) {
	logicalOffset = w.w.Size()
	// Close the log. This writes an EOF trailer signifying the end of the file
	// and syncs it to disk. The caller must close the previous log before
	// creating the new log file, otherwise a crash could leave both logs with
	// unclean tails, and DB.Open will treat the previous log as corrupt.
	err = w.w.Close()
	w.m.mu.Lock()

// Copyright 2012 The LevelDB-Go and Pebble Authors. All rights reserved. Use
// of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be found in
// the LICENSE file.

package pebble

// JobID identifies a job (like a compaction). Job IDs are passed to event
// listener notifications and act as a mechanism for tying together the events
// and log messages for a single job such as a flush, compaction, or file
// ingestion. Job IDs are not serialized to disk or used for correctness.
type JobID int

// newJobIDLocked returns a new JobID; DB.mu must be held.
func (d *DB) newJobIDLocked() JobID {
	res := d.mu.nextJobID
	d.mu.nextJobID++
	return res
}

func (d *DB) newJobID() JobID {
	d.mu.Lock()
	defer d.mu.Unlock()
	return d.newJobIDLocked()
}

   360				// migrations may drop and re-acquire the mutex.
   361				ratcheting bool

   494	// nextSeqNum returns the batch "sequence number" that will be given to the next
   495	// key written to the batch. During iteration keys within an indexed batch are
   496	// given a sequence number consisting of their offset within the batch combined
   497	// with the base.SeqNumBatchBit bit. These sequence numbers are only
   498	// used during iteration, and the keys are assigned ordinary sequence numbers
   499	// when the batch is committed.
   500	func (b *Batch) nextSeqNum() base.SeqNum {
   501		return base.SeqNum(len(b.data)) | base.SeqNumBatchBit
   502	}
   503	
   504	func (b *Batch) release() {
   505		if b.db == nil {
   506			// The batch was not created using newBatch or newIndexedBatch, or an error
   507			// was encountered. We don't try to reuse batches that encountered an error
   508			// because they might be stuck somewhere in the system and attempting to
   509			// reuse such batches is a recipe for onerous debugging sessions. Instead,
   510			// let the GC do its job.
   511			return
   512		}
   513		b.db = nil
   514	
   515		// NB: This is ugly (it would be cleaner if we could just assign a Batch{}),
   516		// but necessary so that we can use atomic.StoreUint32 for the Batch.applied
   517		// field. Without using an atomic to clear that field the Go race detector
   518		// complains.
   519		b.reset()
   520		b.comparer = nil
   521	
   522		if b.index == nil {
   523			batchPool.Put(b)
   524		} else {
   525			b.index, b.rangeDelIndex, b.rangeKeyIndex = nil, nil, nil
   526			indexedBatchPool.Put((*indexedBatch)(unsafe.Pointer(b)))
   527		}
   528	}

  1619	func (b *Batch) reset() {
  1620		// Zero out the struct, retaining only the fields necessary for manual
  1621		// reuse.
  1622		b.batchInternal = batchInternal{
  1623			data:     b.data,
  1624			comparer: b.comparer,

  1632				// If the capacity of the buffer is larger than our maximum
  1633				// retention size, don't re-use it. Let it be GC-ed instead.
  1634				// This prevents the memory from an unusually large batch from
  1635				// being held on to indefinitely.

  1637			} else {

  1639				b.data = b.data[:batchrepr.HeaderLen]

  1641			}

  1648	func (b *Batch) grow(n int) {
  1649		newSize := len(b.data) + n
  1650		if uint64(newSize) >= maxBatchSize {
  1651			panic(ErrBatchTooLarge)
  1652		}
  1653		if newSize > cap(b.data) {
  1654			newCap := 2 * cap(b.data)
  1655			for newCap < newSize {
  1656				newCap *= 2
  1657			}
  1658			newData := rawalloc.New(len(b.data), newCap)
  1659			copy(newData, b.data)
  1660			b.data = newData
  1661		}
  1662		b.data = b.data[:newSize]
  1663	}

  1669	// SeqNum returns the batch sequence number which is applied to the first
  1670	// record in the batch. The sequence number is incremented for each subsequent
  1671	// record. It returns zero if the batch is empty.
  1672	func (b *Batch) SeqNum() base.SeqNum {

  1674			b.init(batchrepr.HeaderLen)
  1675		}
  1676		return batchrepr.ReadSeqNum(b.data)
  1677	}

  1540	
  1541	// Close closes the batch without committing it.
  1542	func (b *Batch) Close() error {
  1543		// The storage engine commit pipeline may retain a pointer to b.data beyond
  1544		// when Commit() returns. This is possible when configured for WAL failover;
  1545		// we don't know if we might need to read the batch data again until the
  1546		// batch has been durably synced [even if the committer doesn't care to wait
  1547		// for the sync and Sync()=false].
  1548		//
  1549		// We still want to recycle these batches. The b.lifecycle atomic negotiates
  1550		// the batch's lifecycle. If the commit pipeline still might read b.data,
  1551		// b.lifecycle will be nonzeroed [the low bits hold a ref count].
  1552		for {
  1553			v := b.lifecycle.Load()
  1554			switch {
  1555			case v == 0:
  1556				// A zero value indicates that the commit pipeline has no
  1557				// outstanding references to the batch. The commit pipeline is
  1558				// required to acquire a ref synchronously, so there is no risk that
  1559				// the commit pipeline will grab a ref after the call to release. We
  1560				// can simply release the batch.
  1561				b.release()
  1562				return nil
  1563			case (v & batchClosedBit) != 0:
  1564				// The batch has a batchClosedBit: This batch has already been closed.
  1565				return ErrClosed
  1566			default:
  1567				// There's an outstanding reference. Set the batch released bit so
  1568				// that the commit pipeline knows it should release the batch when
  1569				// it unrefs.
  1570				if b.lifecycle.CompareAndSwap(v, v|batchClosedBit) {
  1571					return nil
  1572				}
  1573				// CAS Failed—this indicates the outstanding reference just
  1574				// decremented (or the caller illegally closed the batch twice).
  1575				// Loop to reload.
  1576			}
  1577		}
  1578	}
  1579	
  1580	// Indexed returns true if the batch is indexed (i.e. supports read
  1581	// operations).
  1582	func (b *Batch) Indexed() bool {
  1583		return b.index != nil
  1584	}
  1585	
  1586	// init ensures that the batch data slice is initialized to meet the
  1587	// minimum required size and allocates space for the batch header.
  1588	func (b *Batch) init(size int) {
  1589		b.opts.ensureDefaults()
  1590		n := b.opts.initialSizeBytes
  1591		for n < size {
  1592			n *= 2
  1593		}
  1594		if cap(b.data) < n {
  1595			b.data = rawalloc.New(batchrepr.HeaderLen, n)
  1596		}
  1597		b.data = b.data[:batchrepr.HeaderLen]
  1598		clear(b.data) // Zero the sequence number in the header
  1599	}
  1600	
  1601	// Reset resets the batch for reuse. The underlying byte slice (that is
  1602	// returned by Repr()) may not be modified. It is only necessary to call this
  1603	// method if a batch is explicitly being reused. Close automatically takes are
  1604	// of releasing resources when appropriate for batches that are internally
  1605	// being reused.
  1606	func (b *Batch) Reset() {
  1607		// In some configurations (WAL failover) the commit pipeline may retain
  1608		// b.data beyond a call to commit the batch. When this happens, b.lifecycle
  1609		// is nonzero (see the comment above b.lifecycle). In this case it's unsafe
  1610		// to mutate b.data, so we discard it. Note that Reset must not be called on
  1611		// a closed batch, so v > 0 implies a non-zero ref count and not
  1612		// batchClosedBit being set.
  1613		if v := b.lifecycle.Load(); v > 0 {
  1614			b.data = nil
  1615		}
  1616		b.reset()
  1617	}

  1639				b.data = b.data[:batchrepr.HeaderLen]

  1643		if b.index != nil {
  1644			b.index.Init(&b.data, b.comparer.Compare, b.comparer.AbbreviatedKey)
  1645		}
  1646	}

   269	type batchInternal struct {
   270		// Data is the wire format of a batch's log entry:
   271		//   - 8 bytes for a sequence number of the first batch element,
   272		//     or zeroes if the batch has not yet been applied,
   273		//   - 4 bytes for the count: the number of elements in the batch,
   274		//     or "\xff\xff\xff\xff" if the batch is invalid,
   275		//   - count elements, being:
   276		//     - one byte for the kind
   277		//     - the varint-string user key,
   278		//     - the varint-string value (if kind != delete).
   279		// The sequence number and count are stored in little-endian order.
   280		//
   281		// The data field can be (but is not guaranteed to be) nil for new
   282		// batches. Large batches will set the data field to nil when committed as
   283		// the data has been moved to a flushableBatch and inserted into the queue of
   284		// memtables.
   285		data     []byte
   286		comparer *base.Comparer
   287		opts     batchOptions
   288	
   289		// An upper bound on required space to add this batch to a memtable.
   290		// Note that although batches are limited to 4 GiB in size, that limit
   291		// applies to len(data), not the memtable size. The upper bound on the
   292		// size of a memtable node is larger than the overhead of the batch's log
   293		// encoding, so memTableSize is larger than len(data) and may overflow a
   294		// uint32.
   295		memTableSize uint64
   296	
   297		// The db to which the batch will be committed. Do not change this field
   298		// after the batch has been created as it might invalidate internal state.
   299		// Batch.memTableSize is only refreshed if Batch.db is set. Setting db to
   300		// nil once it has been set implies that the Batch has encountered an error.
   301		db *DB
   302	
   303		// The count of records in the batch. This count will be stored in the batch
   304		// data whenever Repr() is called.
   305		count uint64
   306	
   307		// The count of range deletions in the batch. Updated every time a range
   308		// deletion is added.
   309		countRangeDels uint64
   310	
   311		// The count of range key sets, unsets and deletes in the batch. Updated
   312		// every time a RANGEKEYSET, RANGEKEYUNSET or RANGEKEYDEL key is added.
   313		countRangeKeys uint64
   314	
   315		// A deferredOp struct, stored in the Batch so that a pointer can be returned
   316		// from the *Deferred() methods rather than a value.
   317		deferredOp DeferredBatchOp
   318	
   319		// An optional skiplist keyed by offset into data of the entry.
   320		index         *batchskl.Skiplist
   321		rangeDelIndex *batchskl.Skiplist
   322		rangeKeyIndex *batchskl.Skiplist
   323	
   324		// Fragmented range deletion tombstones. Cached the first time a range
   325		// deletion iterator is requested. The cache is invalidated whenever a new
   326		// range deletion is added to the batch. This cache can only be used when
   327		// opening an iterator to read at a batch sequence number >=
   328		// tombstonesSeqNum. This is the case for all new iterators created over a
   329		// batch but it's not the case for all cloned iterators.
   330		tombstones       []keyspan.Span
   331		tombstonesSeqNum base.SeqNum
   332	
   333		// Fragmented range key spans. Cached the first time a range key iterator is
   334		// requested. The cache is invalidated whenever a new range key
   335		// (RangeKey{Set,Unset,Del}) is added to the batch. This cache can only be
   336		// used when opening an iterator to read at a batch sequence number >=
   337		// tombstonesSeqNum. This is the case for all new iterators created over a
   338		// batch but it's not the case for all cloned iterators.
   339		rangeKeys       []keyspan.Span
   340		rangeKeysSeqNum base.SeqNum
   341	
   342		// The flushableBatch wrapper if the batch is too large to fit in the
   343		// memtable.
   344		flushable *flushableBatch
   345	
   346		// minimumFormatMajorVersion indicates the format major version required in
   347		// order to commit this batch. If an operation requires a particular format
   348		// major version, it ratchets the batch's minimumFormatMajorVersion. When
   349		// the batch is committed, this is validated against the database's current
   350		// format major version.
   351		minimumFormatMajorVersion FormatMajorVersion
   352	
   353		// Synchronous Apply uses the commit WaitGroup for both publishing the
   354		// seqnum and waiting for the WAL fsync (if needed). Asynchronous
   355		// ApplyNoSyncWait, which implies WriteOptions.Sync is true, uses the commit
   356		// WaitGroup for publishing the seqnum and the fsyncWait WaitGroup for
   357		// waiting for the WAL fsync.
   358		//
   359		// TODO(sumeer): if we find that ApplyNoSyncWait in conjunction with
   360		// SyncWait is causing higher memory usage because of the time duration
   361		// between when the sync is already done, and a goroutine calls SyncWait
   362		// (followed by Batch.Close), we could separate out {fsyncWait, commitErr}
   363		// into a separate struct that is allocated separately (using another
   364		// sync.Pool), and only that struct needs to outlive Batch.Close (which
   365		// could then be called immediately after ApplyNoSyncWait). commitStats
   366		// will also need to be in this separate struct.
   367		commit    sync.WaitGroup
   368		fsyncWait sync.WaitGroup
   369	
   370		commitStats BatchCommitStats
   371	
   372		commitErr error
   373	
   374		// Position bools together to reduce the sizeof the struct.
   375	
   376		// ingestedSSTBatch indicates that the batch contains one or more key kinds
   377		// of InternalKeyKindIngestSST, InternalKeyKindIngestSSTWithBlobs, or
   378		// InternalKeyKindExcise. If the batch contains key kinds of IngestSST*/Excise
   379		// then it will only contain key kinds of IngestSST/Excise.
   380		ingestedSSTBatch bool
   381	
   382		// committing is set to true when a batch begins to commit. It's used to
   383		// ensure the batch is not mutated concurrently. It is not an atomic
   384		// deliberately, so as to avoid the overhead on batch mutations. This is
   385		// okay, because under correct usage this field will never be accessed
   386		// concurrently. It's only under incorrect usage the memory accesses of this
   387		// variable may violate memory safety. Since we don't use atomics here,
   388		// false negatives are possible.

  1532		if err := d.closed.Load(); err != nil {
  1533			panic(err)
  1534		}
  1535		d.compactionSchedulers.Wait()
  1536		// Compactions can be asynchronously started by the CompactionScheduler
  1537		// calling d.Schedule. When this Unregister returns, we know that the
  1538		// CompactionScheduler will never again call a method on the DB. Note that
  1539		// this must be called without holding d.mu.
  1540		d.compactionScheduler.Unregister()
  1541		// Lock the commit pipeline for the duration of Close. This prevents a race
  1542		// with makeRoomForWrite. Rotating the WAL in makeRoomForWrite requires
  1543		// dropping d.mu several times for I/O. If Close only holds d.mu, an
  1544		// in-progress WAL rotation may re-acquire d.mu only once the database is
  1545		// closed.
  1546		//
  1547		// Additionally, locking the commit pipeline makes it more likely that
  1548		// (illegal) concurrent writes will observe d.closed.Load() != nil, creating
  1549		// more understable panics if the database is improperly used concurrently
  1550		// during Close.
  1551		d.commit.mu.Lock()

  1553		d.mu.Lock()
  1554		defer d.mu.Unlock()
  1555		// Check that the DB is not closed again. If there are two concurrent calls

   390	// WriteOptions hold the optional per-query parameters for Set and Delete
   391	// operations.
   392	//
   393	// Like Options, a nil *WriteOptions is valid and means to use the default
   394	// values.
   395	type WriteOptions struct {
   396		// Sync is whether to sync writes through the OS buffer cache and down onto
   397		// the actual disk, if applicable. Setting Sync is required for durability of
   398		// individual write operations but can result in slower writes.
   399		//
   400		// If false, and the process or machine crashes, then a recent write may be
   401		// lost. This is due to the recently written data being buffered inside the
   402		// process running Pebble. This differs from the semantics of a write system
   403		// call in which the data is buffered in the OS buffer cache and would thus
   404		// survive a process crash.
   405		//
   406		// The default value is true.
   407		Sync bool
   408	}
   409	
   410	// Sync specifies the default write options for writes which synchronize to
   411	// disk.
   412	var Sync = &WriteOptions{Sync: true}
   413	
   414	// NoSync specifies the default write options for writes which do not
   415	// synchronize to disk.
   416	var NoSync = &WriteOptions{Sync: false}
   417	
   418	// GetSync returns the Sync value or true if the receiver is nil.
   419	func (o *WriteOptions) GetSync() bool {
   420		return o == nil || o.Sync
   421	}

	Sync bool

	// propagated to EventListener.BatchDurable notifications for the commit of
	// this write. It can be used to correlate durable commit callbacks with the
	// write that triggered them.
	CommitCorrelationID uint64

# Add method after GetSync
old2='''// GetSync returns the Sync value or true if the receiver is nil.
func (o *WriteOptions) GetSync() bool {
	return o == nil || o.Sync
}
'''
new2=old2+'''
// GetCommitCorrelationID returns the CommitCorrelationID value or 0 if the
// receiver is nil.
func (o *WriteOptions) GetCommitCorrelationID() uint64 {
	if o == nil {
		return 0
	}

   395	type WriteOptions struct {
   396		// Sync is whether to sync writes through the OS buffer cache and down onto
   397		// the actual disk, if applicable. Setting Sync is required for durability of
   398		// individual write operations but can result in slower writes.
   399		//
   400		// If false, and the process or machine crashes, then a recent write may be
   401		// lost. This is due to the recently written data being buffered inside the
   402		// process running Pebble. This differs from the semantics of a write system
   403		// call in which the data is buffered in the OS buffer cache and would thus
   404		// survive a process crash.
   405		//
   406		// The default value is true.
   407		Sync bool
   408	
   409		// CommitCorrelationID is an optional user-provided identifier that is
   410		// propagated to EventListener.BatchDurable notifications for the commit of
   411		// this write. It can be used to correlate durable commit callbacks with the
   412		// write that triggered them.
   413		CommitCorrelationID uint64
   414	}
   415	
   416	// Sync specifies the default write options for writes which synchronize to
   417	// disk.
   418	var Sync = &WriteOptions{Sync: true}
   419	
   420	// NoSync specifies the default write options for writes which do not
   421	// synchronize to disk.
   422	var NoSync = &WriteOptions{Sync: false}
   423	
   424	// GetSync returns the Sync value or true if the receiver is nil.
   425	func (o *WriteOptions) GetSync() bool {
   426		return o == nil || o.Sync
   427	}
   428	
   429	// GetCommitCorrelationID returns the CommitCorrelationID value or 0 if the
   430	// receiver is nil.
   431	func (o *WriteOptions) GetCommitCorrelationID() uint64 {

   433			return 0
   434		}
