type BatchDurableInfo struct {
	// JobID is an identifier for the commit job. It can be used with

	// SeqNum is the sequence number of the first record in the batch.

func (i BatchDurableInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i BatchDurableInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	if i.Err != nil {
		w.Printf("[JOB %d] batch durable error: %s", redact.Safe(i.JobID), i.Err)
		return
	}

		redact.Safe(i.JobID), redact.Safe(i.SeqNum), redact.Safe(i.SyncDuration),
		redact.Safe(i.ApplyDuration))
}

// fired after the WAL sync for a Sync commit completes.

	// JobID is an identifier for the commit job. It can be used with

	// SeqNum is the sequence number of the first record in the batch.

}

func (i BatchDurableInfo) String() string {
	return redact.StringWithoutMarkers(i)
}

// SafeFormat implements redact.SafeFormatter.
func (i BatchDurableInfo) SafeFormat(w redact.SafePrinter, _ rune) {
	if i.Err != nil {
		w.Printf("[JOB %d] batch durable error: %s", redact.Safe(i.JobID), i.Err)
		return
	}
	w.Printf("[JOB %d] batch durable seqnum=%d sync=%s apply=%s",
		redact.Safe(i.JobID), redact.Safe(i.SeqNum), redact.Safe(i.SyncDuration),
		redact.Safe(i.ApplyDuration))
}
'''.split('\n')

for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip()=='// EventListener provides hooks':
        lines = lines[:i] + type_lines + lines[i:]
        break
else:
    raise SystemExit('marker1 not found')

# 2. Insert BatchDurable field after the BackgroundError field in EventListener struct.
# Find the first occurrence of 'BackgroundError func(error)' (the struct field, not tee).
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip()=='BackgroundError func(error)':
        # Only insert if it's the struct field: next non-empty line is "// BlobFileCreated"
        j=i+1
        while j<len(lines) and lines[j].strip()=='':
            j+=1
        if j<len(lines) and lines[j].strip().startswith('// BlobFileCreated'):
            field_lines = [
                '',
                '\t// BatchDurable is invoked after the WAL sync for a Sync commit completes.',
                '\t// It is invoked even if the WAL sync fails.',
                '\tBatchDurable func(BatchDurableInfo)',
            ]
            lines = lines[:i+1] + field_lines + lines[i+1:]
            break
else:
    raise SystemExit('field anchor not found')

# 3. Add TeeEventListener forwarding after BackgroundError tee block.
for i,l in enumerate(lines):
    if l.strip()=='BackgroundError: func(err error) {':
        # find closing '},'
        j=i+1
        while j<len(lines) and lines[j].strip()!= '},':
            j+=1
        tee_lines = [
            '\t\tBatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) {',
            '\t\t\tif a.BatchDurable != nil {',
            '\t\t\t\ta.BatchDurable(info)',
            '\t\t\t}',
            '\t\t\tif b.BatchDurable != nil {',
            '\t\t\t\tb.BatchDurable(info)',
            '\t\t\t}',
            '\t\t},',
        ]
        lines = lines[:j+1] + tee_lines + lines[j+1:]
        break
else:
    raise SystemExit('tee anchor not found')

open(p,'w').write('\n'.join(lines))
print('done')

// SyncOptions has non-nil Done and Err when fsync is requested, else both are
// nil.
type SyncOptions struct {
	Done *sync.WaitGroup
	Err  *error
	// OnSyncComplete is invoked when the requested fsync completes, after Err
	// has been set and before Done is signaled. It is invoked at most once per
	// WriteRecord call that requested a sync. It may be nil.
	OnSyncComplete func()
}

// Writer writes to a virtual WAL. A Writer in standalone mode maps to a
// single record.LogWriter. In failover mode, it can failover across multiple
// physical log files.
type Writer interface {
	// WriteRecord writes a complete record. The record is asynchronously

cd /app && nl -ba record/log_writer.go | sed -n '57,110p' && nl -ba record/log_writer.go | sed -n '140,230p'
OUTPUT:
    57	// syncQueue is a lock-free fixed-size single-producer, single-consumer
    58	// queue. The single-producer can push to the head, and the single-consumer can
    59	// pop multiple values from the tail. Popping calls Done() on each of the
    60	// available *sync.WaitGroup elements.
    61	type syncQueue struct {
    62		// headTail packs together a 32-bit head index and a 32-bit tail index. Both
    63		// are indexes into slots modulo len(slots)-1.
    64		//
    65		// tail = index of oldest data in queue
    66		// head = index of next slot to fill
    67		//
    68		// Slots in the range [tail, head) are owned by consumers.  A consumer
    69		// continues to own a slot outside this range until it nils the slot, at
    70		// which point ownership passes to the producer.
    71		//
    72		// The head index is stored in the most-significant bits so that we can
    73		// atomically add to it and the overflow is harmless.
    74		headTail atomic.Uint64
    75	
    76		// slots is a ring buffer of values stored in this queue. The size must be a
    77		// power of 2. A slot is in use until the tail index has moved beyond it.
    78		slots [SyncConcurrency]syncSlot
    79	
    80		// blocked is an atomic boolean which indicates whether syncing is currently
    81		// blocked or can proceed. It is used by the implementation of
    82		// min-sync-interval to block syncing until the min interval has passed.
    83		blocked atomic.Bool
    84	}
    85	
    86	const dequeueBits = 32
    87	
    88	// unpack extracts the head and tail indices from a 64-bit unsigned integer.
    89	func (q *syncQueue) unpack(ptrs uint64) (head, tail uint32) {
    90		const mask = 1<<dequeueBits - 1
    91		head = uint32((ptrs >> dequeueBits) & mask)
    92		tail = uint32(ptrs & mask)
    93		return head, tail
    94	}
    95	
    96	func (q *syncQueue) push(wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error) {
    97		ptrs := q.headTail.Load()
    98		head, tail := q.unpack(ptrs)
    99		if (tail+uint32(len(q.slots)))&(1<<dequeueBits-1) == head {
   100			panic("pebble: queue is full")
   101		}
   102	
   103		slot := &q.slots[head&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
   104		slot.wg = wg
   105		slot.err = err
   106	
   107		// Increment head. This passes ownership of slot to dequeue and acts as a
   108		// store barrier for writing the slot.
   109		q.headTail.Add(1 << dequeueBits)
   110	}
   140	func (q *syncQueue) pop(head, tail uint32, err error, queueSemChan chan struct{}) error {
   141		if tail == head {
   142			// Queue is empty.
   143			return nil
   144		}
   145	
   146		for ; tail != head; tail++ {
   147			slot := &q.slots[tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
   148			wg := slot.wg
   149			if wg == nil {
   150				return errors.Errorf("nil waiter at %d", errors.Safe(tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)))
   151			}
   152			*slot.err = err
   153			slot.wg = nil
   154			slot.err = nil
   155			// We need to bump the tail count before releasing the queueSemChan
   156			// semaphore as releasing the semaphore can cause a blocked goroutine to
   157			// acquire the semaphore and enqueue before we've "freed" space in the
   158			// queue.
   159			q.headTail.Add(1)
   160			wg.Done()
   161			// Is always non-nil in production, unless using wal package for WAL
   162			// failover.
   163			if queueSemChan != nil {
   164				<-queueSemChan
   165			}
   166		}
   167	
   168		return nil
   169	}
   170	
   171	// pendingSyncs abstracts out the handling of pending sync requests. In
   172	// standalone mode the implementation is a thin wrapper around syncQueue. In
   173	// the mode where the LogWriter can be subject to failover, there is no queue
   174	// kept in the LogWriter and the signaling to those waiting for sync is
   175	// handled in the wal package.
   176	//
   177	// To avoid heap allocations due to the use of this interface, the parameters
   178	// and return values follow some strict rules:
   179	//   - The PendingSync parameter can be reused by the caller after push returns.
   180	//     The implementation should be a pointer backed by a struct that is already
   181	//     heap allocated, which the caller can reuse for the next push call.
   182	//   - The pendingSyncSnapshot return value must be backed by the pendingSyncs
   183	//     implementation, so calling snapshotForPop again will cause the previous
   184	//     snapshot to be overwritten.
   185	type pendingSyncs interface {
   186		push(PendingSync)
   187		setBlocked()
   188		clearBlocked()
   189		empty() bool
   190		snapshotForPop() pendingSyncsSnapshot
   191		pop(snap pendingSyncsSnapshot, err error) error
   192	}
   193	
   194	type pendingSyncsSnapshot interface {
   195		empty() bool
   196	}
   197	
   198	// PendingSync abstracts the sync specification for a record queued on the
   199	// LogWriter. The only implementations are provided in this package since
   200	// syncRequested is not exported.
   201	type PendingSync interface {
   202		syncRequested() bool
   203	}
   204	
   205	// The implementation of pendingSyncs in standalone mode.
   206	type pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue struct {
   207		syncQueue
   208		syncQueueLen    *base.GaugeSampleMetric
   209		snapshotBacking syncQueueSnapshot
   210		// See the comment for LogWriterConfig.QueueSemChan.
   211		queueSemChan chan struct{}
   212	}
   213	
   214	var _ pendingSyncs = &pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue{}
   215	
   216	func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) push(ps PendingSync) {
   217		ps2 := ps.(*pendingSyncForSyncQueue)
   218		q.syncQueue.push(ps2.wg, ps2.err)
   219	}
   220	
   221	func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) snapshotForPop() pendingSyncsSnapshot {
   222		head, tail, realLength := q.syncQueue.load()
   223		q.snapshotBacking = syncQueueSnapshot{
   224			head: head,
   225			tail: tail,
   226		}
   227		q.syncQueueLen.AddSample(int64(realLength))
   228		return &q.snapshotBacking
   229	}
   230

   231	func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) pop(snap pendingSyncsSnapshot, err error) error {

   242		return s.head == s.tail

// SyncRecordWithCallback is like SyncRecord, but the provided callback is
// invoked when the requested sync completes, after err has been set and before
// the WaitGroup is signaled. The callback must be nil if wg is nil.
func (w *LogWriter) SyncRecordWithCallback(
	p []byte, wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error, callback func(),
) (logSize int64, err2 error) {
	w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking = pendingSyncForSyncQueue{
		wg:       wg,
		err:      err,
		callback: callback,
	}
	return w.SyncRecordGeneralized(p, &w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking)
}

type syncSlot struct {
	wg       *sync.WaitGroup
	err      *error
	callback func()
}

func (q *syncQueue) push(wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error, callback func()) {
	ptrs := q.headTail.Load()
	head, tail := q.unpack(ptrs)
	if (tail+uint32(len(q.slots)))&(1<<dequeueBits-1) == head {
		panic("pebble: queue is full")
	}

	slot := &q.slots[head&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
	slot.wg = wg
	slot.err = err
	slot.callback = callback

	// Increment head. This passes ownership of slot to dequeue and acts as a
	// store barrier for writing the slot.
	q.headTail.Add(1 << dequeueBits)
}
	for ; tail != head; tail++ {
		slot := &q.slots[tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)]
		wg := slot.wg
		if wg == nil {
			return errors.Errorf("nil waiter at %d", errors.Safe(tail&uint32(len(q.slots)-1)))
		}
		*slot.err = err
		callback := slot.callback
		slot.wg = nil
		slot.err = nil
		slot.callback = nil
		// We need to bump the tail count before releasing the queueSemChan
		// semaphore as releasing the semaphore can cause a blocked goroutine to
		// acquire the semaphore and enqueue before we've "freed" space in the
		// queue.
		q.headTail.Add(1)
		if callback != nil {
			callback()
		}
		wg.Done()
		// Is always non-nil in production, unless using wal package for WAL
		// failover.
		if queueSemChan != nil {
	snapshotBacking syncQueueSnapshot
	// See the comment for LogWriterConfig.QueueSemChan.
	queueSemChan chan struct{}
}

var _ pendingSyncs = &pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue{}

func (q *pendingSyncsWithSyncQueue) push(ps PendingSync) {
	ps2 := ps.(*pendingSyncForSyncQueue)
	q.syncQueue.push(ps2.wg, ps2.err, ps2.callback)
}
}

func (s *syncQueueSnapshot) empty() bool {
	return s.head == s.tail
}

// The implementation of pendingSync in standalone mode.
type pendingSyncForSyncQueue struct {
	wg       *sync.WaitGroup
	err      *error
	callback func()
}

	return logSize, err
}

// SyncRecord writes a complete record. If wg != nil the record will be
// asynchronously persisted to the underlying writer and done will be called on
// the wait group upon completion. Returns the offset just past the end of the
// record.
// External synchronisation provided by commitPipeline.mu.
func (w *LogWriter) SyncRecord(
	p []byte, wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error,
) (logSize int64, err2 error) {
	return w.SyncRecordWithCallback(p, wg, err, nil)
}

// SyncRecordWithCallback is like SyncRecord, but the provided callback is
// invoked when the requested sync completes, after err has been set and before
// the WaitGroup is signaled. The callback must be nil if wg is nil.
func (w *LogWriter) SyncRecordWithCallback(
	p []byte, wg *sync.WaitGroup, err *error, callback func(),
) (logSize int64, err2 error) {
	w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking = pendingSyncForSyncQueue{
		wg:       wg,
		err:      err,
		callback: callback,
	}
	return w.SyncRecordGeneralized(p, &w.pendingSyncForSyncQueueBacking)
}

   895	func (d *DB) commitWrite(b *Batch, syncWG *sync.WaitGroup, syncErr *error) (*memTable, error) {
   896		var size int64
   897		repr := b.Repr()
   898	
   899		if b.flushable != nil {
   900			// We have a large batch. Such batches are special in that they don't get
   901			// added to the memtable, and are instead inserted into the queue of
   902			// memtables. The call to makeRoomForWrite with this batch will force the
   903			// current memtable to be flushed. We want the large batch to be part of
   904			// the same log, so we add it to the WAL here, rather than after the call
   905			// to makeRoomForWrite().
   906			//
   907			// Set the sequence number since it was not set to the correct value earlier
   908			// (see comment in newFlushableBatch()).
   909			b.flushable.setSeqNum(b.SeqNum())
   910			if !d.opts.DisableWAL {
   911				var err error
   912				size, err = d.mu.log.writer.WriteRecord(repr, wal.SyncOptions{Done: syncWG, Err: syncErr}, b)
   913				if err != nil {
   914					panic(err)
   915				}
   916			}
   917		}
   918	
   919		var err error
   920		// Grab a reference to the memtable. We don't hold DB.mu, but we do hold
   921		// d.commit.mu. It's okay for readers of d.mu.mem.mutable to only hold one of
   922		// d.commit.mu or d.mu, because memtable rotations require holding both.
   923		mem := d.mu.mem.mutable
   924		// Batches which contain keys of kind InternalKeyKindIngestSST or InternalKeyKindIngestSSTWithBlobs will
   925		// never be applied to the memtable, so we don't need to make room for
   926		// write.
   927		if !b.ingestedSSTBatch {
   928			// Flushable batches will require a rotation of the memtable regardless,
   929			// so only attempt an optimistic reservation of space in the current
   930			// memtable if this batch is not a large flushable batch.
   931			if b.flushable == nil {
   932				err = d.mu.mem.mutable.prepare(b)
   933			}
   934			if b.flushable != nil || err == arenaskl.ErrArenaFull {
   935				// Slow path.
   936				// We need to acquire DB.mu and rotate the memtable.
   937				func() {
   938					d.mu.Lock()
   939					defer d.mu.Unlock()
   940					err = d.makeRoomForWrite(b)
   941					mem = d.mu.mem.mutable
   942				}()
   943			}
   944		}
   945		if err != nil {
   946			return nil, err
   947		}
   948		if d.opts.DisableWAL {
   949			return mem, nil
   950		}
   951		d.logBytesIn.Add(uint64(len(repr)))
   952	
   953		if b.flushable == nil {
   954			size, err = d.mu.log.writer.WriteRecord(repr, wal.SyncOptions{Done: syncWG, Err: syncErr}, b)
   955			if err != nil {
   956				panic(err)
   957			}
   958		}
   959	
   960		d.logSize.Store(uint64(size))

            '\t\t}',
            '\t}',
            '',
        ]
        out.extend(block)
        continue
    # Replace WriteRecord SyncOptions literals

        i+=1
        continue

    i+=1

func (d *DB) batchSyncCompleteCallback(b *Batch) func() {
	return func() {
		d.durabilityTracker.onBatchSyncComplete(b)

// Copyright 2025 The LevelDB-Go and Pebble Authors. All rights reserved. Use
// of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be found in
// the LICENSE file.

package pebble

import (
	"context"
	"sync"
	"sync/atomic"
	"time"

	"github.com/cockroachdb/errors"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/base"
)

const (
	// durabilityJobRetention is the number of recent commit job IDs retained by
	// the durability tracker for use by DB.WaitForJobDurability.
	durabilityJobRetention = 4096
	// maxDurabilityNotifiers bounds the number of outstanding durability
	// notification subscriptions (from DB.DurabilityNotify and the durability
	// wait APIs). When the bound is reached, new subscriptions receive an
	// immediate non-nil error.
	maxDurabilityNotifiers = 4096
)

var (
	errDurabilityUnknownJob        = errors.New("pebble: unknown durability job ID")
	errDurabilityExpiredJob        = errors.New("pebble: expired durability job ID")
	errDurabilityTooManySubscriber = errors.New("pebble: too many outstanding durability notifications")
)

// DurabilityStats contains a snapshot of the DB's batch durability state.
type DurabilityStats struct {
	// HighestDurableSeqNum is the highest sequence number (exclusive) that has
	// been made durable by a Sync commit.
	HighestDurableSeqNum base.SeqNum
	// FirstErr is the first WAL sync error encountered, or nil.
	FirstErr error
	// PendingWaiters is the number of goroutines currently blocked in the
	// durability wait APIs.
	PendingWaiters int64
	// TotalDurableCommits is the total number of Sync commits whose WAL sync
	// completed successfully.
	TotalDurableCommits uint64
	// TotalFailedCommits is the total number of Sync commits whose WAL sync
	// failed.
	TotalFailedCommits uint64
	// CumulativeSyncDuration is the cumulative wall-clock time spent waiting for
	// WAL syncs to complete.
	CumulativeSyncDuration time.Duration
	// MaxSyncDuration is the maximum wall-clock time spent waiting for a single
	// WAL sync to complete.
	MaxSyncDuration time.Duration
}

type jobDurabilityRecord struct {
	jobID  int64
	seqNum base.SeqNum
}

type durabilityNotifier struct {
	seqNum  base.SeqNum
	ch      chan error
	pending bool
}

// durabilityTracker tracks the durability state of Sync commits. It is used to
// implement the DB durability wait APIs and to accumulate durability metrics.
type durabilityTracker struct {
	mu sync.Mutex

	highestDurableSeqNum  base.SeqNum
	firstErr              error
	totalDurableCommits   uint64
	totalFailedCommits    uint64
	cumulativeSyncDuration time.Duration
	maxSyncDuration       time.Duration
	closed                bool
	pendingWaiters        int64

	nextJobIDAtomic atomic.Int64
	jobHistory      []jobDurabilityRecord
	jobHistoryHead  int

	notifiers []*durabilityNotifier

	// batchDurableConfigured indicates whether EventListener.BatchDurable is
	// configured. When false, Metrics.DurableCommitCount and
	// Metrics.DurableCommitDuration are not accumulated.
	batchDurableConfigured bool
}

func newDurabilityTracker(batchDurableConfigured bool) *durabilityTracker {
	return &durabilityTracker{batchDurableConfigured: batchDurableConfigured}
}

// nextJobID assigns a new commit job ID for a Sync commit and records it in the
// bounded job history. It is safe for concurrent use.
func (t *durabilityTracker) nextJobID(seqNum base.SeqNum) int64 {
	jobID := t.nextJobIDAtomic.Add(1)
	rec := jobDurabilityRecord{jobID: jobID, seqNum: seqNum}
	t.mu.Lock()
	if len(t.jobHistory) < durabilityJobRetention {
		t.jobHistory = append(t.jobHistory, rec)
	} else {
		t.jobHistory[t.jobHistoryHead] = rec
		t.jobHistoryHead = (t.jobHistoryHead + 1) % len(t.jobHistory)
	}
	t.mu.Unlock()
	return jobID
}

// onBatchSyncComplete is invoked when the WAL sync for a Sync commit completes,
// whether successfully or with an error.
func (t *durabilityTracker) onBatchSyncComplete(b *Batch) {
	b.syncDuration = b.syncStart.Elapsed()
	seqNumEnd := b.durableSeqNum + base.SeqNum(b.keyCount)
	err := b.commitErr

	t.mu.Lock()
	if err != nil {
		t.totalFailedCommits++
		if t.firstErr == nil {
			t.firstErr = err
		}
	} else {
		t.totalDurableCommits++
		if seqNumEnd > t.highestDurableSeqNum {
			t.highestDurableSeqNum = seqNumEnd
		}
	}
	if b.syncDuration > 0 {
		t.cumulativeSyncDuration += b.syncDuration
		if b.syncDuration > t.maxSyncDuration {
			t.maxSyncDuration = b.syncDuration
		}
	}
	t.signalLocked()
	t.mu.Unlock()
}

// signalLocked notifies all outstanding notifiers whose target has been
// reached, or all notifiers if an error or close has occurred. The removed
// notifiers' pending waiter count is decremented.
func (t *durabilityTracker) signalLocked() {
	if t.closed {
		for _, n := range t.notifiers {
			n.ch <- ErrClosed
			if n.pending {
				t.pendingWaiters--
			}
		}
		t.notifiers = nil
		return
	}
	if t.firstErr != nil {
		for _, n := range t.notifiers {
			n.ch <- t.firstErr
			if n.pending {
				t.pendingWaiters--
			}
		}
		t.notifiers = nil
		return
	}
	kept := t.notifiers[:0]
	for _, n := range t.notifiers {
		if n.seqNum <= t.highestDurableSeqNum {
			n.ch <- nil
			if n.pending {
				t.pendingWaiters--
			}
		} else {
			kept = append(kept, n)
		}
	}
	t.notifiers = kept
}

// close unblocks all waiters and notifiers with ErrClosed.
func (t *durabilityTracker) close() {
	t.mu.Lock()
	t.closed = true
	t.signalLocked()
	t.mu.Unlock()
}

// registerLocked registers a notifier for the given seqNum. If the target is
// already satisfied, it returns a nil channel. If the target can never be
// satisfied, it returns an error. If pending is true, the notifier counts
// towards PendingWaiters.
func (t *durabilityTracker) registerLocked(seqNum base.SeqNum, pending bool) (chan error, error) {
	if seqNum <= t.highestDurableSeqNum {
		return nil, nil
	}
	if t.firstErr != nil {
		return nil, t.firstErr
	}
	if t.closed {
		return nil, ErrClosed
	}
	if len(t.notifiers) >= maxDurabilityNotifiers {
		return nil, errDurabilityTooManySubscriber
	}
	ch := make(chan error, 1)
	t.notifiers = append(t.notifiers, &durabilityNotifier{seqNum: seqNum, ch: ch, pending: pending})
	if pending {
		t.pendingWaiters++
	}
	return ch, nil
}

// unregister removes a notifier that has not yet been signaled. It is a no-op
// if the notifier was already removed by signalLocked.
func (t *durabilityTracker) unregister(ch chan error, pending bool) {
	t.mu.Lock()
	for i, n := range t.notifiers {
		if n.ch == ch {
			t.notifiers = append(t.notifiers[:i], t.notifiers[i+1:]...)
			if pending {
				t.pendingWaiters--
			}
			break
		}
	}
	t.mu.Unlock()
}

// waitForSeqNum blocks until seqNum is durable, an error is latched, the DB is
// closed, or ctx is canceled.
func (t *durabilityTracker) waitForSeqNum(ctx context.Context, seqNum base.SeqNum) error {
	t.mu.Lock()
	ch, err := t.registerLocked(seqNum, true)
	t.mu.Unlock()
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if ch == nil {
		return nil
	}
	if ctx == nil {
		err = <-ch
		t.unregister(ch, true)
		return err
	}
	select {
	case err := <-ch:
		t.unregister(ch, true)
		return err
	case <-ctx.Done():
		// Durability/close errors take precedence over context cancellation.
		select {
		case err := <-ch:
			t.unregister(ch, true)
			return err
		default:
		}
		t.unregister(ch, true)
		return ctx.Err()
	}
}

// lookupJob returns the seqNum associated with a commit job ID.
func (t *durabilityTracker) lookupJob(jobID int) (base.SeqNum, error) {
	t.mu.Lock()
	defer t.mu.Unlock()
	if jobID <= 0 {
		return 0, errDurabilityUnknownJob
	}
	jid := int64(jobID)
	next := t.nextJobIDAtomic.Load()
	if jid >= next {
		return 0, errDurabilityUnknownJob
	}
	n := len(t.jobHistory)
	for i := 0; i < n; i++ {
		rec := t.jobHistory[(t.jobHistoryHead+i)%n]
		if rec.jobID == jid {
			return rec.seqNum, nil
		}
	}
	// The job is not in the history. If the history is full and the job ID is
	// older than the retention window, it has expired. Otherwise the job ID was
	// never seen.
	if n == durabilityJobRetention && jid < next-int64(durabilityJobRetention) {
		return 0, errDurabilityExpiredJob
	}
	return 0, errDurabilityUnknownJob
}

// notify registers a durability notification subscription and returns a
// receive-only channel that delivers nil once the seqNum is durable, or a
// non-nil error on WAL sync failure or DB close.
func (t *durabilityTracker) notify(seqNum base.SeqNum) <-chan error {
	t.mu.Lock()
	ch, err := t.registerLocked(seqNum, false)
	t.mu.Unlock()
	if err != nil {
		c := make(chan error, 1)
		c <- err
		return c
	}
	if ch == nil {
		c := make(chan error, 1)
		c <- nil
		return c
	}
	return ch
}

// durableState returns the highest durable sequence number and the first
// latched WAL sync error.
func (t *durabilityTracker) durableState() (base.SeqNum, error) {
	t.mu.Lock()
	defer t.mu.Unlock()
	return t.highestDurableSeqNum, t.firstErr
}

// stats returns a snapshot of the durability stats.
func (t *durabilityTracker) stats() DurabilityStats {
	t.mu.Lock()
	defer t.mu.Unlock()
	return DurabilityStats{
		HighestDurableSeqNum:   t.highestDurableSeqNum,
		FirstErr:               t.firstErr,
		PendingWaiters:         t.pendingWaiters,
		TotalDurableCommits:    t.totalDurableCommits,
		TotalFailedCommits:     t.totalFailedCommits,
		CumulativeSyncDuration: t.cumulativeSyncDuration,
		MaxSyncDuration:        t.maxSyncDuration,
	}
}

// durableMetrics returns the durable commit count and cumulative sync duration,
// which are only accumulated when BatchDurable is configured.
func (t *durabilityTracker) durableMetrics() (uint64, time.Duration) {
	t.mu.Lock()
	defer t.mu.Unlock()
	return t.totalDurableCommits, t.cumulativeSyncDuration
}

// WaitForDurability blocks until the provided sequence number is durable. A
// sequence number is durable once a Sync commit whose sequence number range
// includes it has synced the WAL. A sequence number of zero always succeeds.
//
// All waiters are unblocked with an error when the DB is closed. When WAL is
// disabled, this method returns nil immediately.
func (d *DB) WaitForDurability(seqNum base.SeqNum) error {
	return d.WaitForDurabilityContext(context.Background(), seqNum)
}

// WaitForDurabilityContext is like WaitForDurability, but accepts a context.
// Durability and close errors take precedence over context cancellation.
func (d *DB) WaitForDurabilityContext(ctx context.Context, seqNum base.SeqNum) error {
	if d.opts.DisableWAL {
		return nil
	}
	if ctx == nil {
		ctx = context.Background()
	}
	return d.durabilityTracker.waitForSeqNum(ctx, seqNum)
}

// WaitForDurabilityBatch blocks until every sequence number in the provided
// slice is durable. A nil or empty slice returns immediately.
func (d *DB) WaitForDurabilityBatch(seqNums []base.SeqNum) error {
	return d.WaitForDurabilityBatchContext(context.Background(), seqNums)
}

// WaitForDurabilityBatchContext is like WaitForDurabilityBatch, but accepts a
// context. Durability and close errors take precedence over context
// cancellation.
func (d *DB) WaitForDurabilityBatchContext(ctx context.Context, seqNums []base.SeqNum) error {
	if len(seqNums) == 0 {
		return nil
	}
	if d.opts.DisableWAL {
		return nil
	}
	var max base.SeqNum
	for _, seqNum := range seqNums {
		if seqNum > max {
			max = seqNum
		}
	}
	return d.WaitForDurabilityContext(ctx, max)
}

// WaitForJobDurability blocks until the Sync commit identified by the provided
// commit job ID is durable. The job ID is delivered through
// EventListener.BatchDurable's BatchDurableInfo.JobID.
//
// Job IDs that fall outside the bounded retention window return an "expired"
// error. Job IDs that were never seen, and a zero job ID, return an "unknown"
// error.
func (d *DB) WaitForJobDurability(jobID int) error {
	return d.WaitForJobDurabilityContext(context.Background(), jobID)
}

// WaitForJobDurabilityContext is like WaitForJobDurability, but accepts a
// context. Durability and close errors take precedence over context
// cancellation.
func (d *DB) WaitForJobDurabilityContext(ctx context.Context, jobID int) error {
	if d.opts.DisableWAL {
		return nil
	}
	seqNum, err := d.durabilityTracker.lookupJob(jobID)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	return d.WaitForDurabilityContext(ctx, seqNum)
}

// DurableState returns the highest durable sequence number and the first
// latched WAL sync error, if any.
func (d *DB) DurableState() (base.SeqNum, error) {
	return d.durabilityTracker.durableState()
}

// DurabilityNotify returns a pre-filled receive-only channel that delivers nil
// once the provided sequence number is durable, or a non-nil error on WAL sync
// failure or DB close. The number of outstanding subscriptions is bounded;
// callers beyond the bound receive a pre-filled channel with an immediate
// non-nil error.
func (d *DB) DurabilityNotify(seqNum base.SeqNum) <-chan error {
	if d.opts.DisableWAL {
		ch := make(chan error, 1)
		ch <- nil
		return ch
	}
	return d.durabilityTracker.notify(seqNum)
}

// DurabilityStats returns a snapshot of the durability state.
func (d *DB) DurabilityStats() DurabilityStats {
	return d.durabilityTracker.stats()
}

// finishBatchDurable is invoked once a Sync commit has completed both applying
// to the memtable and syncing the WAL. It invokes EventListener.BatchDurable if
// configured, exactly once per Sync commit.
func (d *DB) finishBatchDurable(b *Batch) {
	if b == nil || !b.syncCommit || !d.durabilityTracker.batchDurableConfigured {
		return
	}
	if !b.durableFired.CompareAndSwap(false, true) {
		return
	}
	info := BatchDurableInfo{
		JobID:          int(b.durableJobID),
		SeqNum:         b.durableSeqNum,
		Err:            b.commitErr,
		ApplyDuration:  b.applyDuration,
		SyncDuration:   b.syncDuration,
		CorrelationID:  b.correlationID,
		BatchSize:      b.batchSize,
		KeyCount:       b.keyCount,
	}
	d.opts.EventListener.BatchDurable(info)
}

// try repeatedly calls f, sleeping between calls with exponential back-off,
// until f returns a nil error or the total sleep time is greater than or equal
// to maxTotalSleep. It always calls f at least once.
func try(initialSleep, maxTotalSleep time.Duration, f func() error) error {
	totalSleep := time.Duration(0)
	for d := initialSleep; ; d *= 2 {
		time.Sleep(d)
		totalSleep += d
		if err := f(); err == nil || totalSleep >= maxTotalSleep {
			return err
		}
	}
}

		time.Sleep(1 * time.Millisecond)

cd /app && cat > durability_test.go <<'EOF'
// Copyright 2025 The LevelDB-Go and Pebble Authors. All rights reserved. Use
// of this source code is governed by a BSD-style license that can be found in
// the LICENSE file.

package pebble

import (
	"context"
	"strings"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/cockroachdb/errors"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/base"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/vfs"
	"github.com/stretchr/testify/require"
)

func openDurabilityTestDB(t *testing.T, el *EventListener) *DB {
	t.Helper()
	opts := &Options{FS: vfs.NewMem()}
	if el != nil {
		opts.EventListener = el
	}
	d, err := Open("", opts)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	t.Cleanup(func() { require.NoError(t, d.Close()) })
	return d
}

func TestBatchDurableEvent(t *testing.T) {
	infos := make(chan BatchDurableInfo, 10)
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, &EventListener{
		BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { infos <- info },
	})

	// Non-sync commit must not trigger BatchDurable.
	require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte("nosync"), []byte("1"), NoSync))

	b := d.NewBatch()
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("b"), []byte("2"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, d.Apply(b, &WriteOptions{Sync: true, CommitCorrelationID: 42}))
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())

	select {
	case info := <-infos:
		require.Greater(t, info.JobID, 0)
		require.Equal(t, uint64(42), info.CorrelationID)
		require.NoError(t, info.Err)
		require.Greater(t, info.BatchSize, 0)
		require.Equal(t, uint32(2), info.KeyCount)
		require.Greater(t, info.ApplyDuration, time.Duration(0))
		require.Greater(t, info.SyncDuration, time.Duration(0))
		// WaitForJobDurability should succeed for the delivered job ID.
		require.NoError(t, d.WaitForJobDurability(info.JobID))
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out waiting for BatchDurable event")
	}

	// Exactly one event for one sync commit.
	select {
	case <-infos:
		t.Fatal("unexpected extra BatchDurable event")
	case <-time.After(50 * time.Millisecond):
	}
}

func TestBatchDurableApplyNoSyncWait(t *testing.T) {
	infos := make(chan BatchDurableInfo, 1)
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, &EventListener{
		BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { infos <- info },
	})
	b := d.NewBatch()
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, d.ApplyNoSyncWait(b, Sync))
	// The event should only fire after SyncWait.
	select {
	case <-infos:
		t.Fatal("BatchDurable fired before SyncWait")
	case <-time.After(50 * time.Millisecond):
	}
	require.NoError(t, b.SyncWait())
	select {
	case info := <-infos:
		require.NoError(t, info.Err)
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out waiting for BatchDurable event")
	}
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())
}

func TestWaitForDurability(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurability(0))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurabilityBatch(nil))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurabilityBatch([]base.SeqNum{}))

	b := d.NewBatch()
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("b"), []byte("2"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, d.Apply(b, Sync))
	seqNum := b.SeqNum()
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())

	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurability(seqNum))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurability(seqNum+1))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurabilityBatch([]base.SeqNum{seqNum, seqNum + 1}))

	high, err := d.DurableState()
	require.NoError(t, err)
	require.GreaterOrEqual(t, high, seqNum+2)

	// A waiter for a far-future sequence number should block until closed.
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurabilityContext(ctx, seqNum+1000))
}

func TestWaitForDurabilityWaits(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	done := make(chan error, 1)
	go func() {
		done <- d.WaitForDurability(100000)
	}()
	// Give the waiter a chance to block.
	time.Sleep(50 * time.Millisecond)
	st := d.DurabilityStats()
	require.Equal(t, int64(1), st.PendingWaiters)

	// A sync commit does not cover seqnum 100000, so the waiter must still be
	// blocked.
	require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), Sync))
	select {
	case <-done:
		t.Fatal("waiter unblocked before sequence number was durable")
	case <-time.After(50 * time.Millisecond):
	}
	// Close unblocks the waiter with an error.
	require.NoError(t, d.Close())
	select {
	case err := <-done:
		require.Error(t, err)
		require.True(t, errors.Is(err, ErrClosed))
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("waiter not unblocked on close")
	}
}

func TestWaitForJobDurabilityErrors(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	err := d.WaitForJobDurability(0)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "unknown")

	err = d.WaitForJobDurability(1)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "unknown")

	err = d.WaitForJobDurability(123456789)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "unknown")
}

func TestWaitForJobDurabilityExpired(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	// Generate enough sync commits to overflow the job history retention window.
	for i := 0; i < durabilityJobRetention+10; i++ {
		require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte{byte(i)}, []byte("1"), Sync))
	}
	err := d.WaitForJobDurability(1)
	require.Error(t, err)
	require.Contains(t, err.Error(), "expired")
}

func TestWaitForJobDurability(t *testing.T) {
	var jobID atomic.Int64
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, &EventListener{
		BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { jobID.Store(int64(info.JobID)) },
	})
	b := d.NewBatch()
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), nil))
	require.NoError(t, d.Apply(b, Sync))
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())
	require.Greater(t, jobID.Load(), int64(0))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForJobDurability(int(jobID.Load())))
}

func TestDurabilityNotify(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)

	// Already-durable seqnum returns nil immediately.
	select {
	case err := <-d.DurabilityNotify(0):
		require.NoError(t, err)
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out")
	}

	// Future seqnum delivers nil once covered by a sync commit.
	ch := d.DurabilityNotify(100)
	b := d.NewBatch()
	require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), nil))
	// Set a high seqnum artificially? Instead use a real batch and wait for its
	// seqnum range.
	require.NoError(t, d.Apply(b, Sync))
	seqNum := b.SeqNum()
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())
	ch2 := d.DurabilityNotify(seqNum)
	select {
	case err := <-ch2:
		require.NoError(t, err)
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out waiting for durability notify")
	}
	_ = ch // ch for seqnum 100 will only fire on close
}

func TestDurabilityNotifyBound(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	chans := make([]<-chan error, 0, maxDurabilityNotifiers+1)
	for i := 0; i < maxDurabilityNotifiers; i++ {
		chans = append(chans, d.DurabilityNotify(1<<30))
	}
	// The next subscription exceeds the bound and gets an immediate error.
	ch := d.DurabilityNotify(1<<30)
	select {
	case err := <-ch:
		require.Error(t, err)
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("expected immediate non-nil error for excess subscriber")
	}
	_ = chans
}

func TestDurabilityStats(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	st := d.DurabilityStats()
	require.Equal(t, base.SeqNum(0), st.HighestDurableSeqNum)
	require.NoError(t, st.FirstErr)
	require.Equal(t, int64(0), st.PendingWaiters)
	require.Equal(t, uint64(0), st.TotalDurableCommits)
	require.Equal(t, uint64(0), st.TotalFailedCommits)

	require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), Sync))
	st = d.DurabilityStats()
	require.Equal(t, uint64(1), st.TotalDurableCommits)
	require.Equal(t, uint64(0), st.TotalFailedCommits)
	require.Greater(t, st.HighestDurableSeqNum, base.SeqNum(0))
	require.Greater(t, st.CumulativeSyncDuration, time.Duration(0))
}

func TestDisableWALWaitAPIs(t *testing.T) {
	opts := &Options{FS: vfs.NewMem(), DisableWAL: true}
	d, err := Open("", opts)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	defer func() { require.NoError(t, d.Close()) }()

	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurability(12345))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForDurabilityBatch([]base.SeqNum{1, 2, 3}))
	require.NoError(t, d.WaitForJobDurability(12345))
	select {
	case err := <-d.DurabilityNotify(12345):
		require.NoError(t, err)
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out")
	}
}

func TestDurabilityMetrics(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, &EventListener{
		BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) {},
	})
	require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), Sync))
	m := d.Metrics()
	require.Equal(t, uint64(1), m.DurableCommitCount)
	require.Greater(t, m.DurableCommitDuration, time.Duration(0))

	// Without BatchDurable configured, the metrics stay zero.
	d2 := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	require.NoError(t, d2.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), Sync))
	m2 := d2.Metrics()
	require.Equal(t, uint64(0), m2.DurableCommitCount)
	require.Equal(t, time.Duration(0), m2.DurableCommitDuration)
}

func TestTeeEventListenerBatchDurable(t *testing.T) {
	var got1, got2 atomic.Int64
	l1 := EventListener{BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { got1.Add(1) }}
	l2 := EventListener{BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { got2.Add(1) }}
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, &func() *EventListener {
		l := TeeEventListener(l1, l2)
		return &l
	}())
	require.NoError(t, d.Set([]byte("a"), []byte("1"), Sync))
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return got1.Load() == 1 && got2.Load() == 1 }, 5*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond)
}

func TestDurabilityCloseUnblocksNotify(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	ch := d.DurabilityNotify(1 << 40)
	require.NoError(t, d.Close())
	select {
	case err := <-ch:
		require.Error(t, err)
		require.True(t, errors.Is(err, ErrClosed))
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("notify not unblocked on close")
	}
}

func TestDurabilityContextCancellation(t *testing.T) {
	d := openDurabilityTestDB(t, nil)
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	done := make(chan error, 1)
	go func() { done <- d.WaitForDurabilityContext(ctx, 1<<30) }()
	time.Sleep(50 * time.Millisecond)
	cancel()
	select {
	case err := <-done:
		require.Error(t, err)
		require.True(t, errors.Is(err, context.Canceled))
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("context cancellation did not unblock waiter")
	}
	// PendingWaiters should return to zero.
	require.Eventually(t, func() bool { return d.DurabilityStats().PendingWaiters == 0 }, 5*time.Second, 10*time.Millisecond)
}

func TestBatchDurableErrMessage(t *testing.T) {
	// Sanity-check that the error messages contain the required substrings.
	require.True(t, strings.Contains(errDurabilityUnknownJob.Error(), "unknown"))
	require.True(t, strings.Contains(errDurabilityExpiredJob.Error(), "expired"))
}

   690				f.ready.Wait()
   691				continue
   692			}
   693			// Found work to do, so no longer idle.
   694			//
   695			// NB: it is safe to read pending before loading from the syncQ since
   696			// mutations to pending require the w.flusher mutex, which is held here.
   697			// There is no risk that someone will concurrently add to pending, so the
   698			// following sequence, which would pick up a syncQ entry without the
   699			// corresponding data, is impossible:
   700			//
   701			// Thread enqueueing       This thread
   702			//                         1. read pending
   703			// 2. add block to pending
   704			// 3. add to syncQ
   705			//                         4. read syncQ
   706			workStartTime := crtime.NowMono()
   707			idleDuration := workStartTime.Sub(idleStartTime)
   708			pending = append(pending[:0], f.pending...)
   709			f.pending = f.pending[:0]
   710			f.metrics.PendingBufferLen.AddSample(int64(len(pending)))
   711	
   712			// Grab the list of sync waiters. Note that syncQueue.load() will return
   713			// 0,0 while we're waiting for the min-sync-interval to expire. This
   714			// allows flushing to proceed even if we're not ready to sync.
   715			snap := f.pendingSyncs.snapshotForPop()
   716	
   717			// Grab the portion of the current block that requires flushing. Note that
   718			// the current block can be added to the pending blocks list after we
   719			// release the flusher lock, but it won't be part of pending. This has to
   720			// be ordered after we get the list of sync waiters from syncQ in order to
   721			// prevent a race where a waiter adds itself to syncQ, but this thread
   722			// picks up the entry in syncQ and not the buffered data.
   723			written := w.block.written.Load()
   724			data := w.block.buf[w.block.flushed:written]
   725			w.block.flushed = written
   726	
   727			fErr := f.err
   728			f.Unlock()
   729			// If flusher has an error, we propagate it to waiters. Note in spite of
   730			// error we consume the pending list above to free blocks for writers.
   731			if fErr != nil {
   732				// NB: pop may invoke ExternalSyncQueueCallback, which is why we have
   733				// called f.Unlock() above. We will acquire the lock again below.
   734				_ = f.pendingSyncs.pop(snap, fErr)
   735				// Update the idleStartTime if work could not be done, so that we don't
   736				// include the duration we tried to do work as idle. We don't bother
   737				// with the rest of the accounting, which means we will undercount.
   738				idleStartTime = crtime.NowMono()
   739				f.Lock()
   740				continue
   741			}
   742			writtenOffset += uint64(len(data))
   743			synced, syncLatency, bytesWritten, err := w.flushPending(data, pending, snap)
   744			f.Lock()
   745			if synced && f.walFileOpHistogram != nil {

package pebble

import (
	"context"
	"sync"
	"sync/atomic"
	"time"

	"github.com/cockroachdb/errors"
	"github.com/cockroachdb/pebble/internal/base"

const (
	// durabilityJobRetention is the number of recent commit job IDs retained by
	// the durability tracker for use by DB.WaitForJobDurability.
	durabilityJobRetention = 4096
	// maxDurabilityNotifiers bounds the number of outstanding durability
	// notification subscriptions (from DB.DurabilityNotify and the durability
	// wait APIs). When the bound is reached, new subscriptions receive an
	// immediate non-nil error.
	maxDurabilityNotifiers = 4096
)

var (
	errDurabilityUnknownJob        = errors.New("pebble: unknown durability job ID")
	errDurabilityExpiredJob        = errors.New("pebble: expired durability job ID")
	errDurabilityTooManySubscriber = errors.New("pebble: too many outstanding durability notifications")
)

// DurabilityStats contains a snapshot of the DB's batch durability state.
type DurabilityStats struct {
	// HighestDurableSeqNum is the highest sequence number (exclusive) that has
	// been made durable by a Sync commit.
	HighestDurableSeqNum base.SeqNum
	// FirstErr is the first WAL sync error encountered, or nil.
	FirstErr error
	// PendingWaiters is the number of goroutines currently blocked in the
	// durability wait APIs.
	PendingWaiters int64
	// TotalDurableCommits is the total number of Sync commits whose WAL sync
	// completed successfully.
	TotalDurableCommits uint64
	// TotalFailedCommits is the total number of Sync commits whose WAL sync
	// failed.
	TotalFailedCommits uint64
	// CumulativeSyncDuration is the cumulative wall-clock time spent waiting for
	// WAL syncs to complete.
	CumulativeSyncDuration time.Duration
	// MaxSyncDuration is the maximum wall-clock time spent waiting for a single
	// WAL sync to complete.
	MaxSyncDuration time.Duration
}

type jobDurabilityRecord struct {
	jobID  int64
	seqNum base.SeqNum
}

type durabilityNotifier struct {
	seqNum  base.SeqNum
	ch      chan error
	pending bool
}

// durabilityTracker tracks the durability state of Sync commits. It is used to
// implement the DB durability wait APIs and to accumulate durability metrics.
type durabilityTracker struct {
	mu sync.Mutex

	highestDurableSeqNum   base.SeqNum
	firstErr               error
	totalDurableCommits    uint64
	totalFailedCommits     uint64
	cumulativeSyncDuration time.Duration
	maxSyncDuration        time.Duration
	closed                 bool
	pendingWaiters         int64

	nextJobIDAtomic atomic.Int64
	jobHistory      []jobDurabilityRecord
	jobHistoryHead  int

	notifiers []*durabilityNotifier

	// batchDurableConfigured indicates whether EventListener.BatchDurable is
	// configured. When false, Metrics.DurableCommitCount and
	// Metrics.DurableCommitDuration are not accumulated.
	batchDurableConfigured bool
}

func newDurabilityTracker(batchDurableConfigured bool) *durabilityTracker {
	return &durabilityTracker{batchDurableConfigured: batchDurableConfigured}
}

// nextJobID assigns a new commit job ID for a Sync commit and records it in the
// bounded job history. It is safe for concurrent use.
func (t *durabilityTracker) nextJobID(seqNum base.SeqNum) int64 {
	jobID := t.nextJobIDAtomic.Add(1)
	rec := jobDurabilityRecord{jobID: jobID, seqNum: seqNum}
	t.mu.Lock()
	if len(t.jobHistory) < durabilityJobRetention {
		t.jobHistory = append(t.jobHistory, rec)
	} else {
		t.jobHistory[t.jobHistoryHead] = rec
		t.jobHistoryHead = (t.jobHistoryHead + 1) % len(t.jobHistory)
	}
	t.mu.Unlock()
	return jobID
}

// onBatchSyncComplete is invoked when the WAL sync for a Sync commit completes,
// whether successfully or with an error.
func (t *durabilityTracker) onBatchSyncComplete(b *Batch) {
	b.syncDuration = b.syncStart.Elapsed()
	seqNumEnd := b.durableSeqNum + base.SeqNum(b.keyCount)
	err := b.commitErr

   694				table.StringWithTupleIndex("level", 5, table.AlignRight, func(tupleIndex int, m *LevelMetrics) string {
   695					if tupleIndex == manifest.NumLevels {
   696						return "total"
   697					}
   698					return fmt.Sprintf("L%d", tupleIndex)
   699				}),
   700				table.Div(),
   701				table.Float("score", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) float64 { return m.Score }),
   702				table.Float("ff", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) float64 { return m.FillFactor }),
   703				table.Float("cff", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) float64 { return m.CompensatedFillFactor }),
   704				table.Div(),
   705				table.Count("tables", 6, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.TablesMoved.Count }),
   706				table.Bytes("size", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.TablesMoved.Bytes }),
   707				table.Div(),
   708				table.Bytes("top", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.MultiLevel.TableBytesInTop }),
   709				table.Bytes("in", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.MultiLevel.TableBytesIn }),
   710				table.Bytes("read", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.MultiLevel.TableBytesRead }),
   711				table.Div(),
   712				table.Bytes("tables", 6, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.TableBytesRead }),
   713				table.Bytes("blob", 5, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.BlobBytesRead }),
   714				table.Div(),
   715				table.Count("tables", 6, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.TablesFlushed.Count + m.TablesCompacted.Count }),
   716				table.Bytes("sstsz", 6, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.TablesFlushed.Bytes + m.TablesCompacted.Bytes }),
   717				table.Bytes("blobsz", 6, table.AlignRight, func(m *LevelMetrics) uint64 { return m.BlobBytesFlushed + m.BlobBytesCompacted }),
   718			)
   719			def.FilterFn = func(tupleIndex int, m *LevelMetrics) (passed bool) {
   720				return !math.IsNaN(m.Score) || m.FillFactor != 0 || m.TablesMoved.Count != 0 || m.MultiLevel.TableBytesInTop != 0 ||
   721					m.MultiLevel.TableBytesIn != 0 || m.MultiLevel.TableBytesRead != 0 || m.BlobBytesRead != 0 ||
   722					m.TablesFlushed.Count != 0 || m.TablesCompacted.Count != 0 || m.BlobBytesFlushed != 0 || m.BlobBytesCompacted != 0
   723			}
   724			return def
   725		}()

   405			infoFunc: func(format string, args ...interface{}) {
   406				infoCount++
   407			},
   408			errorFunc: func(format string, args ...interface{}) {
   409				errorCount++
   410			},
   411			fatalFunc: func(format string, args ...interface{}) {
   412				t.Fatal("Unexpected call to Fatalf")
   413			},
   414		}, &infoCount, &errorCount
   415	}

   435	func testAllCallbacksSetInEventListener(t *testing.T, e EventListener) {
   436		t.Helper()
   437		v := reflect.ValueOf(e)
   438		for i := 0; i < v.NumField(); i++ {
   439			fType := v.Type().Field(i)
   440			fVal := v.Field(i)
   441			require.Equal(t, reflect.Func, fType.Type.Kind(), "unexpected non-func field: %s", fType.Name)
   442			require.False(t, fVal.IsNil(), "unexpected nil field: %s", fType.Name)
   443		}
   444	}

  1787	// TargetFileSize computes the target file size for the given output level.
  1788	func (o *Options) TargetFileSize(outputLevel int, baseLevel int) int64 {
  1789		if outputLevel == 0 {
  1790			return o.TargetFileSizes[0]
  1791		}
  1792		if baseLevel > outputLevel {
  1793			panic(errors.AssertionFailedf("invalid base level %d (output level %d)", errors.Safe(baseLevel), errors.Safe(outputLevel)))
  1794		}
  1795		return o.TargetFileSizes[outputLevel-baseLevel+1]
  1796	}

  1598	// EnsureDefaults ensures that the default values for all options are set if a

  1600	func (o *Options) EnsureDefaults() {

  1627		}

  1630				return ValueSeparationPolicy{

   880		return w.closeInternal(PendingSyncIndex{Index: NoSyncIndex})
   881	}
   882	
   883	// CloseWithLastQueuedRecord is like Close, but optionally accepts a
   884	// lastQueuedRecord, that the caller will be notified about when synced.
   885	func (w *LogWriter) CloseWithLastQueuedRecord(lastQueuedRecord PendingSyncIndex) error {
   886		return w.closeInternal(lastQueuedRecord)
   887	}
   888	
   889	func (w *LogWriter) closeInternal(lastQueuedRecord PendingSyncIndex) error {
   890		f := &w.flusher
   891	
   892		// Emit an EOF trailer signifying the end of this log. This helps readers
   893		// differentiate between a corrupted entry in the middle of a log from
   894		// garbage at the tail from a recycled log file.
   895		w.emitEOFTrailer()
   896	
   897		// Signal the flush loop to close.
   898		f.Lock()
   899		f.close = true
   900		f.ready.Signal()
   901		f.Unlock()
   902	
   903		// Wait for the flush loop to close. The flush loop will not close until all
   904		// pending data has been written or an error occurs.
   905		<-f.closed
   906	
   907		// Sync any flushed data to disk. NB: flushLoop will sync after flushing the
   908		// last buffered data only if it was requested via syncQ, so we need to sync
   909		// here to ensure that all the data is synced.
   910		err := w.flusher.err
   911		var syncLatency time.Duration
   912		if err == nil && w.s != nil {
   913			syncLatency, err = w.syncWithLatency()
   914		}
   915		f.Lock()
   916		if err == nil && f.walFileOpHistogram != nil {
   917			f.walFileOpHistogram.Observe(float64(syncLatency))
   918		}
   919		free := w.free.blocks
   920		f.Unlock()
   921	
   922		// NB: the caller of closeInternal may not care about a non-nil cerr below
   923		// if all queued writes have been successfully written and synced.
   924		if lastQueuedRecord.Index != NoSyncIndex {
   925			w.pendingSyncsBackingIndex.externalSyncQueueCallback(lastQueuedRecord, err)
   926		}
   927		if w.c != nil {
   928			// Measure close latency
   929			closeStart := crtime.NowMono()
   930			cerr := w.c.Close()
   931			if w.flusher.walFileOpHistogram != nil {
   932				w.flusher.walFileOpHistogram.Observe(float64(closeStart.Elapsed()))
   933			}
   934			w.c = nil
   935			err = firstError(err, cerr)
   936		}
   937	
   938		for _, b := range free {
   939			b.flushed = 0
   940			b.written.Store(0)

STEP 182 CALL 1 RETURN 0
COMMAND:
cd /app && cat >> durability_test.go <<'EOF'

func TestBatchDurableLargeBatch(t *testing.T) {
	infos := make(chan BatchDurableInfo, 1)
	opts := &Options{FS: vfs.NewMem(), MemTableSize: 1 << 16}
	opts.EventListener = &EventListener{BatchDurable: func(info BatchDurableInfo) { infos <- info }}
	d, err := Open("", opts)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	defer func() { require.NoError(t, d.Close()) }()

	b := d.NewBatch()
	val := bytes.Repeat([]byte("v"), 1024)
	for i := 0; i < 64; i++ {
		require.NoError(t, b.Set([]byte(fmt.Sprintf("key%02d", i)), val, nil))
	}
	require.NoError(t, d.Apply(b, Sync))
	require.NoError(t, b.Close())

	select {
	case info := <-infos:
		require.NoError(t, info.Err)
		require.Greater(t, info.BatchSize, 1<<16)
		require.Greater(t, info.ApplyDuration, time.Duration(0))
		require.Greater(t, info.SyncDuration, time.Duration(0))
	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
		t.Fatal("timed out waiting for large batch durable event")
	}
}
EOF

open(p,'w').write(s)
print('done')

func TestSyncRecordWithCallback(t *testing.T) {
	mem := vfs.NewMem()
	f, err := mem.Create("log", vfs.WriteCategoryUnspecified)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	w := NewLogWriter(f, 0, LogWriterConfig{
		WriteWALSyncOffsets: func() bool { return false },
	})

	var syncErr error
	var syncWG sync.WaitGroup
	syncWG.Add(1)
	callbackCalled := false
	_, err = w.SyncRecordWithCallback([]byte("hello"), &syncWG, &syncErr, func() {
		callbackCalled = true
	})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	syncWG.Wait()
	require.True(t, callbackCalled)
	require.NoError(t, syncErr)
}

func TestSyncRecordWithCallbackOnError(t *testing.T) {
	mem := vfs.NewMem()
	f, err := mem.Create("log", vfs.WriteCategoryUnspecified)
	require.NoError(t, err)
	injectedErr := errors.New("injected error")
	w := NewLogWriter(syncErrorFile{f, injectedErr}, 0, LogWriterConfig{
		WriteWALSyncOffsets: func() bool { return false },
	})

	var syncErr error
	var syncWG sync.WaitGroup
	syncWG.Add(1)
	callbackCalled := false
	_, err = w.SyncRecordWithCallback([]byte("hello"), &syncWG, &syncErr, func() {
		callbackCalled = true
	})
	require.NoError(t, err)
	syncWG.Wait()
	require.True(t, callbackCalled)
	require.Equal(t, injectedErr, syncErr)
}

// Op describes a filesystem operation.
type Op struct {
	// Kind describes the particular kind of operation being performed.
	Kind OpKind
	// Path is the path of the file of the file being operated on.
	Path string
	// Offset is the offset of an operation. It's set for OpFileReadAt and
	// OpFileWriteAt operations.
	Offset int64
}

// OpKind is an enum describing the type of operation.
type OpKind int

const (
	// OpCreate describes a create file operation.
	OpCreate OpKind = iota
	// OpLink describes a hardlink operation.
	OpLink
	// OpOpen describes a file open operation.
	OpOpen
	// OpOpenDir describes a directory open operation.
	OpOpenDir
	// OpRemove describes a remove file operation.
	OpRemove
	// OpRemoveAll describes a recursive remove operation.
	OpRemoveAll
	// OpRename describes a rename operation.
	OpRename
	// OpReuseForWrite describes a reuse for write operation.
	OpReuseForWrite
	// OpMkdirAll describes a make directory including parents operation.
	OpMkdirAll
	// OpLock describes a lock file operation.
type InjectorFunc func(Op) error

// String implements fmt.Stringer.
func (f InjectorFunc) String() string { return "<opaque func>" }

// MaybeError implements the Injector interface.
func (f InjectorFunc) MaybeError(op Op) error { return f(op) }

// Injector injects errors into FS operations.
type Injector interface {
	fmt.Stringer
	// MaybeError is invoked by an errorfs before an operation is executed. It
	// is passed an enum indicating the type of operation and a path of the
	// subject file or directory. If the operation takes two paths (eg,
	// Rename, Link), the original source path is provided.
	MaybeError(op Op) error
}

// Any returns an injector that injects an error if any of the provided
// injectors inject an error. The error returned by the first injector to return
// an error is used.
func Any(injectors ...Injector) Injector {
	return anyInjector(injectors)
}

type anyInjector []Injector

func (a anyInjector) String() string {
	var sb strings.Builder
	sb.WriteString("(Any")
	for _, inj := range a {
		sb.WriteString(" ")
		sb.WriteString(inj.String())
	}
	sb.WriteString(")")
	return sb.String()
}

STEP 242 CALL 1 RETURN 128
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "db: add batch durability tracking and BatchDurable event

Add an EventListener.BatchDurable callback that fires exactly once per
Sync commit after the WAL sync completes, even on WAL sync failure. The
callback delivers a BatchDurableInfo containing the commit job ID,
sequence number, error, apply and sync durations, correlation ID, batch
size, and key count.

Add a durability tracker to every DB (regardless of whether BatchDurable
is configured) that tracks the highest durable sequence number and first
WAL sync error, and powers new DB methods:
  - WaitForDurability[Context]
  - WaitForDurabilityBatch[Context]
  - WaitForJobDurability[Context]
  - DurableState
  - DurabilityNotify
  - DurabilityStats

All waiters unblock with an error on DB close. When WAL is disabled the
wait APIs and DurabilityNotify return nil immediately. The WAL writer is
extended with an OnSyncComplete callback invoked after the sync completes
(and before the commit's WaitGroup is signaled) in both standalone and
failover modes. The callback is also wired through TeeEventListener.

Expose Metrics.DurableCommitCount and Metrics.DurableCommitDuration,
accumulated only when EventListener.BatchDurable is configured." && git log --oneline -3
